# Refactor bug ledger

Owner, Nov 12 2026: "As you refactor and find these bugs, they should be fixed at some point either by arc close or by landing."

Every bug a refactor step finds gets a row here: where it was found, the step that fixes it, and the deadline, which is either the landing that found it or the close of the current arc. An arc doesn't close while a row due at its close is open. A strict xfail is allowed only as the fail-first test of a queued fix. A fix that changes persisted meaning (scoring, scheduling, projections) follows `docs/algorithm-change-playbook.md`, and related fixes share one successor. Only a genuine design question goes to the owner, with a recommendation.

Status: **open**, **queued** (a step owns it), **fixed** (commit), **owner** (waiting on a decision).

## Due at arc D's close

| L42-R1 | L4-2 adversarial review (blocker) | A grade written by a process holding the predecessor's config, committed after another process completed the curves' activation (then 0.71, now 0.72), recorded no curve, and its epoch stayed missing_capture for good. | `tests/test_grade_curves_capture_integrity.py` (two) | Every applied grade records its curve whatever tag the process loaded. | fixed |
| L42-R2 | L4-2 adversarial review (blocker's wider class) | Every tag-gated write on the grade path (and every other successor gate) reads the algorithm tag from the writing process's loaded `vault.config`. The tag lives in `learnloop.toml`, not in the database, so a process that loaded an older config (the sidecar, the phone host, a CLI, a background job) applies that tag's rules until it reloads, also after another process completed an upgrade. L42-R1 removed the one instance that lost a raw fact (the curve capture no longer reads the tag); the gates that choose between predecessor and successor computations still read it. | none | Open: the activation stores the tag in SQLite and every gate reads it under the writer lock at one seam (a schema change across every gate; not L4-2's). | open, pre-existing class |

The eight workers then held 0.73 to 1.95 GB each (10.3 GB) with the 2 GB of swap full. A worker's memory grows through the run; what grows was not read (candidates: the idle-connection reuse of `tests/connection_reuse.py`, the per-session template vaults, modules and caches each worker accumulates). The lane had been at the edge: about 100 new golden tests tipped it. | the landing selection in one `-n 8` run in a 10 GB scope | Until the growth is found and bounded, the orchestrator's gate runs the lane as two halves (the sorted test files, alternating; each its own `-n 8` run in 10 GB; the two totals are checked against the partition's count): 30,930 of 30,930, no kill. The fix proper: measure a worker's growth by test file, bound the cache that grows, and make CLAUDE.md's gate line true again | open (arc F or the test shape pass T5; the split gate holds meanwhile) |
| D-52 | arc D close fixes, group 3 (D-36's readers) | An owner adjudication (`learnloop grading adjudicate`, `append_adjudication`) sits on the observation it names, and the next rebuild's re-minted observation (D-36), unadjudicated, is the one the authoritative ledger reads, so the adjudication's evidence is gone after one rebuild: on a synthetic vault a confirmed `partial_success` moves the facet row's direct positive mass 2.1743 -> 2.2314 and one `rebuild-derived-state` returns it to 2.1743. A contest flag is lost the same way (by reading). No owner copy holds an adjudication or a flag | `test_rebuild_history_appends::test_an_adjudication_survives_rebuilds[mvp-0.60]` (was a strict xfail), `::test_a_contest_flag_survives_rebuilds`, `::test_an_adjudication_that_flips_the_grade_is_not_taken_for_a_regrade` | D-36's fix: a head an adjudication, a review flag or a quarantine wrote is kept unless the grade changed (a regrade supersedes it, as decided); an adjudication's own direction revision is not a regrade | fixed at `mvp-0.60` with D-36 when this branch lands (`cf96a8c566`, `01cb5925af`) |
| D-53 | arc D close fixes, group 3 (D-36's readers) | `Repository.pending_grade_reviews` (`learnloop grading reviews`) joins every observation's head, not each attempt's newest, so a flagged grade is listed once per rebuild (1, 2, 3 rows after 0, 1, 2 rebuilds on a synthetic vault), and the superseded copies' flags stay listed where an adjudication never reaches the projection | `test_rebuild_history_appends::test_the_review_queue_lists_a_flagged_grade_once_after_rebuilds[mvp-0.59]` (was a strict xfail), `::test_the_observation_of_an_attempt_is_the_newest_whatever_the_query_plan` | Read each attempt's newest observation (`observation_ledger`'s order); `observation_by_attempt` orders by `(created_at, id)` instead of relying on the index; clears the repeats in vaults already rebuilt without a cleanup; not persisted meaning, not gated by the tag | fixed (`22ac042f67`) |
| D-54 | landing gate of the figures steps K10.5 and K10.6 | `tests/test_apply_recovery.py::test_the_sidecar_accept_names_the_outstanding_intent` and `::test_an_undo_with_a_pending_intent_is_refused` failed once in the landing lane: the child their helper forks (`multiprocessing.get_context("fork")`, to crash mid-apply) had not exited after 60 s (`exitcode` None). The xdist worker is multi-threaded when it forks (Python 3.12 warns of it in every run), so the child can inherit a lock another thread held and never finish. Not on the step's diff; 6 of 6 passed on rerun. Seven test files fork this way: `test_vault_lock`, `marker_fakes`, `test_migration_coordinator`, `test_apply_write_ahead`, `test_apply_recovery`, `test_learner_report_superseded`, `test_upgrades`. | D-55 | arc D close fixes, group 3, step b (D-36's proof) | A live answer's dual write stamps its observation 1 to 2 seconds after the attempt row's `created_at`, while replay re-mints at the attempt's `created_at`, so every base re-mint sorts before the live observation and the authoritative ledger reads the live one forever: those attempts are never re-interpreted under a newer calibration, and a practice regrade's grade channel never reaches the projection (its direction does, through grading evidence). 13 of 85 observed attempts in the `linear_algebra` copy, 2 of 3 in `arxiv_v2`, 1 of 3 in `probability`, 0 of 18 on the `mvp-0.17` fixture | the step-b proof (base against branch on the copies), re-run on the final code in step d; no test | D-36's fix reuses that observation and re-reads it, which moves canonical facet cells where the calibration changed since the live grade (7 attempts and 2 attempts); the owner accepted that as the activation of the fix's own tag, `mvp-0.60` (2026-11-17; `docs/reviews/refactor-adfix3.md` steps b-d) | fixed at `mvp-0.60` with D-36 when this branch lands (`cf96a8c566`, `01cb5925af`) |
| D-56 | arc D close fixes, group 3, step b | A vault adjudicated (or flagged) and then rebuilt before D-36's fix keeps the adjudication on a superseded observation, unread by the projection after the fix too; not repaired by the fix. ## From the phase 1 adversarial review (fix round closed 2026-11-13)

All 38 findings are closed in `docs/reviews/refactor-phase-1-review.md` ("Outcome of the fix round"): the rows this review reopened (K1-1, K1-4, K1-6, D-9, D-10, K3.3, D-1, D-5) are fixed there. What the round left open:

Tests in `test_sidecar_stops_serving.py`, `test_exam_session.py` (`refactor-adfix1.md`) | fixed (`e671b6f05e`) |
| R1-2 | FR-C (P6) | A remediation episode whose primed attempt is never taken never ends: no task, no expiry, nothing writes `abandoned`. Until it has a lifetime the routine queue withholds its cold item only while a cold retry is open. | Owner decided 2026-11-20: it ends at the item's next ordinary review. Successor mvp-0.64 (K9.4): the per-attempt sweep ends an untaken `prescribed` or `treatment` episode as `abandoned` once the item of the miss it repairs is due (or had its review since the episode opened), and from the tag `routine_serving_exclusions` reads the wider set (`docs/reviews/algorithm-mvp-0.64.md`) | fixed (mvp-0.64; commit at landing) |

## From arc I (due at arc I's close)

| Id | Found in | Bug or open question | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| I2-1 | I2 step 0 (`docs/reviews/refactor-i2.md` §6) | A queue build with `persist_explanations=False` and a session writes: it records contrast-pair servings (`_apply_contrast_pair_order` with `record=True`) and ends the session's committed probe presentation when it is blocked or not in the composed queue, though the docstring says a read writes nothing. Shown on the base: the contrast-pair vault built with `session_id="sess_read"` and the flag off writes 2 `contrast_pair_servings` rows. Read callers with a session: the sim, the staged comparator (a shadow comparison whose first-written serving row can stand for one the learner never got), the plan prefetch (read-only attach: the contrast write fails and is logged; an invalidation would raise and the prefetch returns "unavailable") and `scripts/voi_cap_report.py`. | Both writes move to the serving build's persist step (`_persist_offer`) and `read_due_queue` replaces the flag; fail-first `tests/test_due_queue_read_builds.py` (`3c189d12f5`) | fixed (`6c7918046e`; `docs/reviews/refactor-i2.md` §8) |
| I2-2 | I2-1's fix (the no-session read golden) | A read build still writes: `Repository.due_followup_tasks`, which the build calls through `pending_followup_practice_items`, marks every due pending task `served`, marks lapsed ones `expired` and releases their cold reservations, on any writable attach. So drawing the facet chart or answering `why` marks a cold retry `served` that nobody was shown. Documented as lazy marks that change nothing the read returns (a read-only attach skips them), and nothing found reads `served` apart from `pending`, but the status no longer means served. | Decide whether the marks belong to the serving build's persist step (rows change for read callers: behaviour track), or rename the status's meaning in its docs | open (arc I close) |

## From RE2 (arc F; due at arc F's close)

| Id | Found in | Bug or open question | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| RE2-1 | RE2 step 0 (`docs/reviews/refactor-re2.md` §2) | The forgetting curve's array form (numpy's `power`, which takes its own SIMD kernel on a CPU with AVX-512) and its scalar form (the C library's `pow`) differ by one ulp on about 5.3% of the inputs a vault produces, so its last bit depends on the machine. It reaches the rollouts' `P(perform)` and vectorized FSRS update (a Monte Carlo over draws, where one ulp is far below the noise but could flip an exact tie), and `daily_plan/objective_inputs`'s two one-value reads, which read the array form: the randomized checks' frozen sensors stored in the plan record and the combiner's trace join, so on those inputs they differ in the last bit from the same retrievability every scalar site reads (the decision receipt's `r`, rounded, hides it except at a rounding boundary). No spec says which is right; the C library's is the reference (`fsrs.py`, py-fsrs). RE2 kept each site's form, bit for bit. | Recommended: accept for the rollouts and say so in `retrievability_array`'s docstring (done); put the two one-value reads on the scalar form with F2, which owns `memory.py`'s arithmetic, under P-shadow (the stored sensors move by at most one ulp). Orchestrator's call. | open (arc F's close) |
| RE2-2 | RE2 step 0 | `daily_plan/router_card.py:548` reads retrievability with the FSRS-6 default weights (no weights passed), while every other read uses the vault's fitted set (`resolve_fsrs_weights`). The card's `recall_now` is shown and bucketed into the LLM router's prompt (`router_retrieval.py:95`), so on a vault that has run `learnloop fit` the card and the router's prompt disagree with the queue, the knowledge map and the decision receipt. None of the three owner copies has a fitted set, so nothing differs today. | Small fix: pass `resolve_fsrs_weights(repository)` (the prompt changes only on a vault with fitted weights; check the identity pins of the router's prompt) | open (arc F's close) |

## From the review of the evidence-packets and AD memory specs (2026-11-17; SR-1 is learner-visible and is fixed first; SR-2 to SR-4 are due with the evidence-packets spec's EPp, and all four before EP3b and ADM2a)

Two independent reviews of `docs/specs/spec_evidence_packets_v1.md` and `docs/specs/spec_ad_memory_v1.md` at `88b10fb90d`; each row was checked against the code at that tip before it was added.

| Id | Found in | Bug or open question | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| SR-1 | Spec review (EP §11) | When the LLM component wins the objective router's draw, its rationale becomes the block's learner-visible explanation (`router/why.py:222-223`, `model_written`; since E2 in `router_objective/choose.py`'s `choose`, where the guard's verdict and `shown` are decided; before E2 `daily_plan/objective_chooser.py:1600-1612`), and `rationale_guard` (`router/why.py:258-284`) checks only sentence count, internal terms, probability-like numbers and cited ids, not content. The card already applies learner protection (no reference solution, key, rubric or unattempted stem: `objective_card.py:9-10`), so what can leak today is a paraphrase of the learner's own earlier answer or its graded feedback ahead of a cold retry, or a miss-cause class before a probe (FG1 §7); hypothesis statements (EP Q3) would widen it. | Design, small: the shown `why` for a decision whose card carries sensitive context is computed from the rule rather than the rationale, or the guard gains a content check against the protected fields; fail-first test plants an answer-bearing entry and exercises the real `why` path before a probe | open (before EP3b) |
| SR-2 | Spec review (propensity logging) | `daily_plan/slot_facts.py:119-130` turns a missing router propensity into `1.0` (`router_propensity=float(propensity) if propensity is not None else 1.0`), and adaptive difficulty multiplies it into the stored pair propensity (`adaptive_difficulty/serving.py:266`), whose own code keeps `None` for an unknown one. A block with no router decision, or decided before propensities were logged, is recorded as a certain choice. | Small fix: pass `None` through and let the pair propensity be `None`; a proven deterministic choice (single option, irreversible) records `1.0` with its reason | fixed by EPp receipt repairs (record [epp-c-receipt-repairs.md](epp-c-receipt-repairs.md)): new SlotFacts v2 carries unknown router propensity as null through the actual AD slot path; v1 numeric records retain their value and version; no historical rows rewritten |
| SR-3 | Spec review (propensity logging) | A randomized cold check whose drawn item is no longer eligible is redrawn among the topic's eligible items (`router_objective/checks.py:321-323`), but the placement detail (`:342-346`) records only `item_how = "redrawn_in_pool"` and the topic probability: not the candidate pool, the redraw's uniform or its conditional item probability, while the slot keeps the original item's `item_propensity`. The redraw's probability cannot be recovered later, since the pool depended on that moment's eligibility. | Small fix: save the redraw's candidate ids, uniform and `1/len(candidates)` and the replaced original in the placement detail | fixed by EPp receipt repairs (record [epp-c-receipt-repairs.md](epp-c-receipt-repairs.md)): new placement detail records the ordered redraw pool, existing uniform, conditional item probability and original/replacement identities separately from the unchanged assignment |
| SR-4 | Spec review (prediction snapshots) | The `ad_threshold@*` forecast receipts store `p_categories: None` (`adaptive_difficulty/serving.py:423`) although the model computes the three category probabilities on the way to `p_bar` (`model.py:150-177`, `expected_success` is category 2 of `category_probabilities`), so the full predicted distribution is lost for every fresh-item serve. | Small fix: store the grid-weighted mixture of the category probabilities, with `p_bar` its category 2 (EPp, `spec_evidence_packets_v1.md` §7.7.3) | fixed by EPp's first part (record [epp-a-prediction-snapshots.md](epp-a-prediction-snapshots.md)): each `ad_threshold@*` receipt stores `p_categories`, computed from the same rows, residual variance, guess floor and weights as `p_bar`; category 2 equals `p_bar` exactly, tested in all three mixture views |
| EPp-1 | EPp's first part (by test) | `scheduling/forecast_receipts.py`'s `clamped_as_of` parses `inputs_as_of` with `parse_utc`, which raises on unreadable text, so `record_receipts` drops the whole batch (every receipt of the call, logged) instead of clamping it to the issue time as it does a missing or future time. No producer is known to pass unreadable text today | Fail-first: `tests/test_epp_prediction_snapshots.py::test_an_unreadable_inputs_time_is_recorded_invalid` (strict xfail); `test_the_status_check_of_the_unreadable_time` pins today's behaviour. Fix: clamp an unreadable time like a future one and record it `invalid` in the snapshot (already computed) | part A fixed; part B stopped for a successor tag under algorithm-change playbook rule 1 (record [epp-1-invalid-receipt-times.md](epp-1-invalid-receipt-times.md)). The scorer now marks an unreadable bound time invalid (`unreadable_time`), preserves every readable score row byte-for-byte, and rebuilds idempotently. The clamp still drops the batch: persisting previously dropped receipts changes persisted meaning, so its strict xfail and characterization remain pending the orchestrator's tag |
| EPp-2 | EPp's first part (a test run of one file) | `tests/test_ad1_fix_round5.py::test_the_draft_keeps_hint_seen_under_its_retry_key[on]` and `[off]` fail with `KeyError: 'start_session'` when the file runs alone (`-n 1`): `tests/ad1_attempt_columns_helpers.py` reads `learnloop_sidecar.registry.METHOD_REGISTRY`, which holds the sidecar's methods only once some other module has imported the handlers, so the test passes only after another test did. Reproduced with this step's source changes reverted (base `3f42da228a`'s source, same tests): not caused by EPp | Small test fix: the helper imports the handler package that registers the methods (as the sidecar does at start) | fixed, test-only (record [epp-1-invalid-receipt-times.md](epp-1-invalid-receipt-times.md)): explicit handler-package registration in the named helper and all 20 other helper modules found by the audit; the round-5 file passes alone at `-n 1` |
| EPp-3 | EPp's second part (measured by its benchmark under the test lane's SQLite shortcuts; low) | Recording the predecessor's prediction on a repeat presentation adds about 14 ms to the serve at a small history and 66 to 80 ms at 242 visible attempts, growing linearly with history: the replay's event load runs even with a warm cache, the attempts are scanned several times, and `z_v1`'s recalibration is computed twice. The receipts decide nothing, so the cost buys only later evaluation | Reuse the serve's own signal vector and replay (one load), or move the capture off the serve path (a queued write that reads as of the serve) | open; before ADM2a turns on, or sooner if a serve is seen to be slow |

## From K5: grading, regrade, coldness and certification (2026-11-18)

An independent review reported fourteen findings. `docs/reviews/refactor-k5.md` verified each against the code at `c1e3706164`; none was refuted. Deadlines fall into three groups. Group (a): fix now, before arc G, for the high plain defects. Group (b): with the step that rewrites the code. Group (c): the owner decides. The record's §10 proposes the batches.

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| K5-1 | K5 (medium) | A model grade with no criterion rows (or with criteria left out) on an ordinary item, off a route the grader calls divergent, scores 0. Validation sums the rows present (`attempts/grading.py:860`). Nothing is unassessed, so the grade is not held (`attempts/attempts.py:4287`, `:4303`), and FSRS records an Again review. A regrade with no rows mints a replacement id and supersedes every live row, a self-grade's included (`attempts/regrade.py:373`, `:549-556`). The valid-alternative gate also reads an omitted criterion as full marks (`attempts/grading_support.py:472`) where validation scores it 0. | `tests/test_blind_grading_contract.py`: an ordinary proposal with no rows on a non-empty rubric is refused (or held). `tests/test_deferred_regrade.py`: a regrade returning no rows leaves the self-grade's rows and the due date. Sparse teach-back still passes | Refuse an empty ordinary proposal at the practice door and in regrade, as an invalid proposal is refused; one omitted-criterion rule. Plain (intake only), S | fixed on `fix/k5-batch2`; its omitted-criterion rule (score over the criteria present) was unsafe and is replaced by ER-1: an omission is refused |
| K5-2 | K5 (high) | The repair feedback says "the repair is confirmed and the factor is closed" (`apps/learnloop-tauri/src/screens/FeedbackScreen.tsx:212`) when the cold check passed but its receipt's `qualifies_as_repair_effect_verification` is False. Ordinary interleaved practice on the same topic before the check does this (`tests/test_coldness_receipt.py:357-391`). `sidecar/handlers/serializers.py:757-764` checks only the budget downgrade, and a failed check is called "did not hold" on the same basis. | `tests/test_sidecar_remediation_surfaces.py`: repair, another item of the topic, then a passed check; the claim is not `repair_confirmed` and `passed` stays true. The same with no final receipt | Gate `repair_confirmed` and `escalated_unrepaired` on the repair claim being True; otherwise a "recorded, not attributable" claim and copy. Plain (display), S. Owner, one line: should a non-attributable pass still close the case and count as repair support (persisted meaning)? | fixed on `fix/k5-batch1`. Owner, Nov 18: yes. A repair followed by ordinary practice and a passed cold check confirms the repair; the stored closure and support stay as they are. The label is now stricter than that rule: K5-16 |
| K5-3 | K5 (medium) | The certificate probe's verdict never reads the coldness receipt: it writes `held` or `failed` (`goals/certification_cold_probe.py:1358-1393`) and only then the receipt (`:1438`). A verified answer whose every criterion is unassessed (correctness 1.0 "for scheduling only") is scored `held`, though repair verification refuses it (`diagnosis/causal_probe_coherence.py:1893-1904`). It affects only `false_certification_rate` (CLI) and the cold-outcome labels. | `tests/test_certification_cold_probe.py`: an all-unassessed verified answer gives `indeterminate`, outside the denominator; the retrieval rule's test follows the owner's choice | Plain part: abstain on an all-unassessed grade. Choice: the receipt's certificate claim forbids any other retrieval in the topic, which a probe weeks later nearly always has. The owner chooses whether the probe measures retention with ordinary practice (keep) or the certificate alone (gate on the claim). Bump `COLD_PROBE_POLICY_VERSION` and a tag, S | fixed at mvp-0.61 on `algo/mvp-0.61` (`62c5947a8d`); landed with the tag (2026-11-19) (the plain part; the retrieval rule stays, owner Nov 18) |
| K5-4 | K5 (low) | Two research readers use weaker coldness rules than the cold claim. The field reader takes a final receipt's unassisted and leakage findings, not its delay (`field/outcomes.py:387-425`, documented at `:390-393`), while placement calls its stricter rule "the field's strict rule" (`placement/state.py:967`). Intervention outcomes check their own window but not another retrieval in the topic (`substrate/intervention_outcomes.py:150-190`). ## From the adversarial review of arc E (2026-11-18)

The review of `914145d0b9..8804080fcf` read source only; each scenario below was reproduced with a failing test before its fix (grading: `docs/reviews/refactor-efix1.md`; router: `docs/reviews/refactor-efix2.md`).

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| ER-1 | Review finding 1 (high) | Full marks with nothing assessed. (a) K5 batch 2 scored an ordinary grade that left criteria out over the criteria present and stored a "Criterion omitted by grader" row marked unassessed for each: 2/2 on setup beside a wrong result left out was 4/4, and feedback called the omitted criterion "not assessed (a different route)". (b) A grade taken over the assessed criteria counted a remainder worth no points as assessed, so the grade was not held, and `assessed_rubric_score` gave its zero denominator a fraction of 1: on the review's rubric (form 0, setup 2, result 2), setup and result marked down on a divergent route with nothing located scored 4/4 unheld, live and on regrade; leaving result out scored 4/4 too. | `tests/test_route_rules_in_grader.py::test_er1_a_grade_that_leaves_out_a_criterion_is_refused`, `::test_er1_nothing_with_points_assessed_is_held_not_full_marks` (live, regrade), `::test_er1_the_rules_treat_a_weightless_remainder_as_nothing_assessed`; `tests/test_blind_grading_contract.py::test_ordinary_grade_requires_every_criterion` (partial; it pinned 4/4) | An ordinary proposal reports every criterion of a non-empty rubric or is refused like an empty one; the rescale and the synthetic rows go. Criteria worth no points left assessed alone join the unverified ones in PF-c's gate and the own-route rule (`grading_support.weightless_remainder`), so the grade is held when nothing validated the answer; `assessed_rubric_score` has no zero-denominator default. Intake only. Not changed: the offline hard-case harness (`diagnosis/grading_hardcases.py` `_production_path`) applies the gate and the rule without validation, so it still scores an omission as unlocated where production now refuses it (its report's fidelity; with the harness's next version) | fixed on `fix/arc-e-review-grading` (`c01b78a57d`); the harness note open |
| ER-2 | Review finding 2 (high; narrower after ER-1) | A row the own-route rule or PF-c marks unassessed (since mvp-0.17) is read by both diagnosis readers as an assessed zero. Trace consistency (`diagnosis/causal_attribution.py` `_trace_consistency_states`) counts the claim "this criterion fell short" as checked and held (`consistent_with_claims`) instead of `claim_criteria_were_not_assessed`; the criterion receipt (`_criterion_receipt`) makes it an assessable, failed criterion. Replay rematerializes the receipt from the stored rows (`attempts/attempts.py`, `materialize_causal_episode` in the apply path; a rebuild of the test's vault calls the reader for the attempt), so the fix moves rebuilt receipts of stored diagnosed attempts. | `tests/test_causal_trace_consistency_p2.py::test_a_criterion_left_unassessed_neither_holds_nor_fails_a_claim` (strict xfail), beside `::test_an_assessed_zero_holds_a_shortfall_claim` | Both readers skip a row `canonical_projection.unassessed_evidence_row` marks: its claim is unverifiable and it is no assessable outcome. A tag (it changes what a rebuild computes) | fixed at mvp-0.61 on `algo/mvp-0.61` (`403eba963a`); landed with the tag (2026-11-19) |
| ER-3 | Review finding 3 (high) | `learnloop grading adjudicate` could commit a head other than the one it printed. At mvp-0.60, a zero-point answer ruled a success (A) and then confirmed (B): B wrote no evidence revision, so the current grade was A's revision; replay's protection looked for B's marker, missed it and appended an automatic interpretation as head, while the command printed B. Below mvp-0.60 its replay re-mints the observation without the ruling (D-52), so the command undid its own ruling. | `tests/test_grading_cli.py::test_a_confirmation_of_an_adjudicated_grade_stays_the_head`, `::test_below_the_grade_channel_fixes_adjudicate_does_not_replay`, `::test_adjudicate_refuses_to_commit_a_head_its_replay_displaced`; `::test_adjudicate_rolls_back_a_replay_that_fails_after_its_reset` (passed before and after) | A ruling that confirms an earlier ruling's revised grade writes that grade again as its own. Below mvp-0.60 the command does not replay and says when derived state will follow. Before it commits, the command requires the ruling to be the head of the observation the ledger reads for the attempt, else it rolls back | fixed on `fix/arc-e-review-grading` (`bcd1940224`) |
| ER-4 | Review finding 4 (high) | E2 and E4 moved three reads of state the job's threads publish ahead of their old points: the LLM's card (the trace's learner statements) and its request (the question set named in the router and decision records) before the pure choice, and `carried_for` before the trace's rows. A card build that ended after the serve's wait, or a carried valuation that landed while the rows were built, was recorded before the arc and dropped after it. No served choice moved. | `tests/test_router_sampling_points.py` (7 cases; on the base the three card cases fail, and a scratch schedule script shows the carry case: `[]` on the base, the record before the arc) | Each read is back at its point before the arc (the card after the draw, `carried_for` inside the trace's assembly after its rows and goal dates, the question set after the trace). The decision's record is built after the trace (`choose.decision_record`); `choose` stays pure. The rationale guard still reads the card's evidence ids before the draw: the job publishes the card before it asks the LLM, so the two reads agree whenever the guard reads them (an argument, not a test) | fixed on `fix/arc-e-review-router` (`9ade08f447`) |
| ER-5 | Review finding 5 (high) | Removing the `replay` config section changed `versions.config` in new `decision_state_receipts`. | none: every reader of the table was checked (`tail_data` reads `state.los` and `pending_reports`, `trace.record` the id, the report counts rows), and none compares `versions.config` | No change. The hash is provenance beside the code revision, not an identity anything compares (`docs/reviews/refactor-efix2.md` §5) | by design |
| ER-6 | Review finding 6 (high) | Imports written before K5-15 could still reach the grader. The legacy fallback's SQL `trim` removes spaces only, so a score-only answer the old importer kept as "\t\n" read as work in deferred and explicit regrade alike. A legacy score-only import seeded with an empty rubric has no import rows, so the explicit-regrade check read NULL as "has work". | `tests/test_deferred_regrade.py::test_a_legacy_import_whose_answer_is_only_whitespace_is_score_only` (three cases), `::test_a_legacy_import_with_no_import_rows_is_score_only` | The fallback trims Python's whitespace (`str.isspace`, pinned by `::test_the_legacy_whitespace_rule_is_pythons`), as `seed_exam_attempts` does. `is_score_only_exam_import` reads the attempt's type: an `exam_evidence` attempt is score-only unless an import row says `learner_work` | fixed on `fix/arc-e-review-grading` (`39b5fa9038`) |
| ER-7 | Review finding 7 (high) | The identity pin C3b pinned the seed's reduced identity under the name "key", not the boundary key the chooser hands its job, and the pinned draws' ordinals hid a swap of `mix_seed`'s arguments. | the two new pins, each failing for its own mutation (dropping `held_reading_map` from the key; swapping the seed's arguments) while every other pin passes | Two pins added, none moved: `C3b.start_key` and `C3m.seed` (production's seed, under `real_draws`) | fixed on `fix/arc-e-review-router` (`8fc9291471`) |
| ER-8 | Review finding 8 (high) | A failure while building the decision's record inside `choose` escaped after the draw, and the engine then served the rule's fallback: a record's construction changed what was served. | `tests/test_router_failure_records.py::test_a_decision_record_that_cannot_be_built_leaves_the_drawn_block_served` (on the base the fallback's block is served), at both build sites, with a drawn block that differs from the fallback's pick | The record is built after the trace, outside `choose` (`objective_chooser._built_record`). When it cannot be built the drawn block is served, the block's record is derived from the router's record, and a `decision_record_build` failure record (type and place, never the message) is written as its child. A failure building the trace or the router's record still falls back, as before the arc | fixed on `fix/arc-e-review-router` (`9ade08f447`, `fae3920269`) |
| ER-9 | Review finding 9 (medium; the rest of D-45) | The carried-valuation thread was started before it was listed, so a concurrent `join_carried` could say none was running; no host's close stopped a carry; a carry was keyed by database path, so a later session's or plan's trace could record it. | `tests/test_carried_valuation_lifecycle.py` (6, with thread barriers; on the base the listing race and the cross-plan and cross-session records fail) | See D-45's fix cell. ## From K6: Reader Ask and retrieval side effects (2026-11-18)

The review is `docs/reviews/refactor-k6.md`; paths in these rows use its short forms (`L/` is `src/learnloop/`, `R/` is `src/learnloop/source_retrieval/`, `S/` is `src/learnloop_sidecar/handlers/`, `M/` is `src/learnloop_mobile/`, `UI/` is `apps/learnloop-tauri/src/`). No test was run by the review: each row's test is to be written and seen to fail before its fix.

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| K6-1 | K6 review (high) | Generated help counts as seen. Submit a substantive practice Ask, disconnect after the provider succeeds, and submit the item from another client. The answered question and reveals already exist. Or start Library Ask and never poll the completed job: its source exposures already exist. The same distinction applies to a phone result refused by its final provenance check. An ordinary retry with a new request identity can add another generated event. `L/tutor/tutor_qa.py:1243–1249,1292–1302,1312–1389`, `R/library_ask.py:668–682`, `S/library_ask.py:292–310,333–354,396–428`, `S/reader.py:224–229`, `L/adaptive_difficulty/reading.py:442–475` | new: preserve the exchange and incurred spend, but assert zero delivered hints, reveals, source exposures and answer-derived warmth until a delivery acknowledgement arrives. Delivering the same result twice should count once. | assign the prepared result a stable display ID. Commit display-dependent effects once at presentation, retaining learner-authored question, paid output and usage at their current boundaries. Apply the same boundary to retrieval citation feedback cleared by a worked-help refusal. | open: with the Decider's DJ1 (display ids) |
| K6-2 | K6 review (high) | Bound pins, unbound answer. From a non-cold item workbench, Ask the library, pin a safe passage and ask the tutor a mechanism question. `follow_up_caller` resolves the item. `OnlyPinned` receives it. The nested `RD.ask` receives only the source provider. The common tutor therefore has no bound item for its hint or overlap, even if the generated answer gives substantial help. `S/library_ask.py:94–144,794–811`, `S/reader_tutor.py:199–241`, `L/tutor/tutor_qa.py:1264–1285,1336`, `tests/test_library_ask_review.py:248,265` | `test_library_ask_review::test_the_pinned_tutor_counts_a_bound_asks_help_on_its_item_once` | pass the same validated `BoundAsk` used by ordinary workbench Reader Ask to `RD.ask`, including the workbench/session identity. Keep the pins-only provider. | fixed on `fix/k6-round-a` (`21cd7ada40`) |
| K6-3 | K6 review (high) | A weaker fallback reaches the prompt and chip. An LO has a current `support` link to a non-exam block that is also the practiced item’s solution. Let normal verification be unavailable. The primary linked span passes the relation and E checks. The fallback’s stronger filter is offered only to followed references. The model can cite the solution beside a harmless sentence. The worked-answer filter sees that sentence, the chip includes the book text, and the `provided_spans is not None` debit branch is skipped. `L/content/authoring/practice_leakage.py:432–479`, `R/tutor.py:349`, `L/tutor/tutor_qa.py:1227–1233,1288–1301,1374–1389,2389–2401,2441–2444,2470–2493`, `S/tutor_qa.py:462–468` | `test_tutor_delivery_fixes::test_a_linked_span_that_is_the_items_own_solution_never_reaches_the_fallback`, `test_tutor_delivery_fixes::test_a_cited_fallback_passage_is_debited_as_a_source_review` | apply the caller’s normal policy to the primary fallback keys before hydration and measure every delivered citation independently of the provider branch. | fixed on `fix/k6-round-a` (`2f6c051931`) |
| K6-4 | K6 review (high) | Current anchor protection does not cover the whole Reader request. On the phone, select an allowed block beside an E-but-not-H unit in a mixed reading. The phone checks only the requested selection up front. Reader context expands neighbors/references under H. The returned manifest can contain those texts even if the tutor cites only the allowed block, and `ask_provenance` does not enumerate all `manifest.source_spans`. Separately, start an allowed branch whose answer quotes another source, protect that source, then continue the branch. Ancestors are reconstructed as question/answer strings. Checking the inherited selection does not check the origin of that earlier prose before sending it to the model. `S/reader.py:143–156,224–244`, `L/reader/reader_dialogue.py:277–312,648–699,722–759,974–1016`, `L/tutor/tutor_qa.py:1018–1055,2518–2552` | `test_mobile_v4_reader::test_the_phones_ask_never_sends_exam_material_in_its_context_or_reply`, `test_tutor_delivery_fixes::test_a_branchs_earlier_answer_from_a_now_held_out_passage_never_reaches_the_tutor` | carry the destination-specific predicate into context assembly. Retain and recheck the source keys of every included ancestor before adding its prose. Build the manifest and final provenance from the admitted context. | fixed on `fix/k6-round-a` (`bba27da874`) |
| K6-5 | K6 review (high) | Reopening a result bypasses a new mark. Ask the library while a passage is allowed. Mark the passage held out. Request organizer notes. With no cached organizer, the worker reads the old keys, loads the text and sends it to the judge. With a cached organizer, it returns the notes immediately. On the phone, complete an allowed Ask, mark its cited source, then poll the completed job or repeat the completed request. The host returns the stored result directly, whereas a fresh history request filters it. `R/library_ask.py:795–810,871–908,935`, `S/library_ask.py:333–354,364–410,510–520`, `S/reader.py:321–333`, `M/runtime.py:357–366,679–685`, `M/host.py:403–407,587–609` | `test_library_ask_review::test_a_mark_after_the_ask_withholds_its_cached_notes_and_its_kept_result`, `test_library_ask_review::test_a_mark_after_the_ask_keeps_its_passages_from_the_organizer`, `test_mobile_v4_reader::test_a_completed_phone_ask_is_withheld_when_its_passage_is_marked_since` | add a current-provenance check to organizer input and all delivery paths for these stored results. Reuse the kept-comparison callback pattern and the phone history predicate, extended as in K6-4. | fixed on `fix/k6-round-a` (`088d9058c7`) |
| K6-6 | K6 review (high) | The reserve detector sees fewer fields than the learner. Reserve a distinctive question. Return a neutral explanation and citation label, with that question reproduced only in a verified excerpt or only in the bridge. Neither field is passed to `_detect_revealed_reserves`. No collision row is written and the eligibility query can still say unseen. `L/tutor/reader_modes.py:569–629,701–707`, `L/reader/reader_dialogue.py:457–505,776–815,895–896`, `L/substrate/activities.py:355–389`, `tests/test_reader_dialogue.py:301–344`, `tests/test_library_ask_review.py:185` | `test_reader_tutor_retrieval::test_a_reserve_shown_only_in_the_excerpt_or_the_bridge_is_burned_once` (excerpt, bridge), `test_reader_tutor_retrieval::test_a_reserve_no_shown_field_reproduces_stays_unseen` | give the reserve detector the final visible text fields and retain which field matched. Keep the caller-declared union and existing fingerprint behavior. | fixed on `fix/k6-round-a` (`d8db9aa3d4`) |
| K6-7 | K6 review (high) | Teacher choices escape withdrawal. Ask for help during an open check. Accept a turn with innocuous message prose and a rendered choice that reproduces the solution. Help replies bypass the ordinary open-solution veto. Withdrawal compares only `turn.message`. Lesson-wide reveal accounting deliberately excludes its own minted check. The learner can then answer the still-open check. `L/teacher/guards.py:241–258`, `L/teacher/service.py:1297–1318,1483–1503,1638–1673,1715–1720`, `UI/screens/LessonScreen.tsx:580`, `tests/test_teacher_te1_lesson.py:194`, `tests/test_teacher_te1_review_fixes.py:473` | `test_teacher_te1_lesson::test_help_that_gives_the_solution_away_in_a_choice_withdraws_the_check` | give the help-withdrawal comparison the same assembled visible text as the accepted-turn display path. Keep the existing rule for ordinary turns and the learner’s legitimate help request. | fixed on `fix/k6-round-a` (`863c12ca99`) |
| K6-8 | K6 review (high) | Compare omits a debit on two successful passage returns. After the post-attempt release window, retrieve an own-source passage in a comparison where only one book verifies. `too_few_sources` still returns a source entry. Alternatively, reuse a stored comparison containing an own passage under the applicable post-attempt policy. Both branches return before the common full own-source debit. The library wrapper can separately record source exposure, which is not the missing item `reveal_events` row. `R/compare.py:539–553,577–583,695–702,872`, `R/find_sources.py:835,896`, `R/library_ask.py:1131–1155,1317–1328`, `S/library_ask.py:375–379` | `test_compare_sources::test_a_reused_comparison_showing_the_items_own_passage_is_debited_like_a_fresh_one`, `test_compare_sources::test_a_one_source_comparison_showing_the_items_own_passage_is_debited` | finalize the item debit from the final displayed passage list, outside the fresh-generation branch. | fixed on `fix/k6-round-a` (`0654795ece`) |

Measure a full-source open at that later action. | fixed on `fix/k6-round-a` (`06efb33bbc`) |

## From G3: the two diagnosis beliefs (2026-11-18; due before arc G's G2)

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| G3-1 | G3, on the live arxiv vault's four answered probes (high) | A probe answer given after feedback was shown, or after a reveal, is refused by the discriminating observation as not independent (`diagnosis/causal_discrimination.py:161-181,249-280,449-482`), but the episode posterior counts it at full weight: its contamination test looks only at hints, tutor help and the attempt type, not at the workflow exposure or the reveal ledger (`diagnosis/probe_evidence.py:181-193,613-640,665-677`). Three of the four real answers are in this state: `updates_belief` and `eligible_for_completion` set, discount 1.0. `instructional/valuation` reads that posterior, so an exposed answer moves the choice of repair and can complete an episode. | `tests/test_probe_evidence.py` (or where the episode observation is tested): a probe answered after "show anyway" and one answered after a reveal write an observation that is contaminated, does not qualify for completion, and moves the posterior by no more than the configured discount; a clean answer is unchanged | The episode observation applies the same exposure test the discriminating observation applies (one function, two callers), so the two records cannot differ on whether an answer was independent. Replay output moves for stored episodes: a tag, S to M | fixed at mvp-0.61 on `algo/mvp-0.61` (`93f0edc1d9`, `939ce40fce`; `d44387d4e3`: an exposed answer updates no belief at all, the held grade's shape, rather than the configured discount); landed with the tag (2026-11-19) |
| G3-2 | G3 (medium, proof infrastructure) | The owner-lane comparison copies (`owner-copies-mvp058`, upgraded on Nov 12 from an October set) predate all of the arxiv vault's November use: they hold 3 attempts where the live vault holds 53, and no probe presentation, follow-up after a miss or walkthrough reconstruction. Every owner-lane proof since (the landing gate's owner lane, P-shadow, the value changes shown for `mvp-0.60`) ran on data that never exercises those paths. | The owner lane's copy script records the newest attempt date of each copy, and the gate prints it beside the live vaults' (read from a fresh backup copy) so a stale set is seen | Refresh the comparison set from backup copies of the live vaults at their current tag; rerun the owner lane and a shadow rebuild to `mvp-0.60` on the fresh set and show the owner the real value changes before they upgrade. Proof infrastructure, S | partly done. The comparison on a fresh set is in [owner-copies-refresh-2026-11-18.md](owner-copies-refresh-2026-11-18.md): `mvp-0.60`'s own change equals what the owner accepted. Still open: six owner-lane test files assume the old set's contents (11 failures on the fresh set: `test_router_r2a_serving.py`, `test_router_r2b_serving.py`, `test_ad23_successor.py`, `test_placement_fixture_copies.py`, `test_plan_follows_path.py`, `test_why_real_cases.py`); when they no longer do, the gate's copy script takes a dated fresh set and prints each copy's newest attempt date |
| G3-3 | mvp-0.61 adversarial review, finding 6 (medium) | `evsi_calibration/live_report.py` reads only a probe observation's `grader_channel_json` and `features_json`: an observation that updates no belief (a held grade, or an exposed answer at mvp-0.61) still has its observed outcome treated as realized, and the report computes a full-weight `posterior(likelihood, prior, emission)` for its information gain and truth probability and an action change from the emission (`summarize_joined`). | an observation with `updates_belief` false (held, or exposed at the tag) adds no entropy drop, no action-change pair and no updated truth probability; a damped one is weighted | Carry the effective admission and weight into the adapter (`_join` with the vault's repository: `probe_episodes` effective view per attempt) and separate "an answer was observed" (calibration of the predicted outcome) from "this answer produced a belief update" (the update metrics, at the effective weight). A report, not a decision: no tag | open: more than a small change (the weighted update and the per-attempt effective view in a report that reads a raw connection); the review's T61-6 |
| G3-4 | The G3 rerun on the owner's upgraded arxiv vault (`refactor-g3.md`, "Rerun on the upgraded vault (2026-11-20)"); aggregates only | On causal-probe episodes the episode posterior cannot move: each presentation's frozen slot map sends every locked hypothesis (the causal ones and the open arm) to the card's `other_or_unknown` row (`probe_families.map_episode_labels_to_slots`: a label the card does not bind falls to the open-set row), so every hypothesis has the same likelihood for every outcome. On all five presentations the selection-time information is 0, the posterior after each of the four answers equals the lock's verbalized prior, and the four episodes completed as `action_equivalent` on that prior. Neither record learned a cause from any answer (the discriminating observation: `no_bundle_matched` four times, one admitted, none closed). G4 as written would make the discriminating observation defer to a posterior that is only the prior | none yet: a causal-probe episode whose card binds no locked hypothesis reports that its instrument cannot separate them (and is not counted as a measured completion); with the hypotheses bound from the presentation's pinned blind bundles, an answer that matches one bundle moves the posterior toward its cause | The owner decides (asked 2026-11-20): close G4 on the skip branch with a doctor warning, or first compile the episode likelihood from the pinned blind bundles (a spec and a tagged behaviour change: selection information becomes non-zero, so probe choice changes). It also bounds G2: at this instrument a probe buys no information whatever its price | open; the owner's call; found by measurement, by design in the code (the abstention rule), so not a regression |

## From the mvp-0.61 review (2026-11-19; the tag's adversarial review, `docs/reviews/algorithm-mvp-0.61.md` "Adversarial review and fix round")

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| T61-1 | mvp-0.61 review, finding 1 (high) | Whether a stored probe answer was exposed depended on the reading time (`reveal_ledger.production_admissibility` read the wall clock for routine displays), an answer's own feedback in its second exposed it on a later read, and FG1c ignored causes shown in the answer's own second | `test_evidence_reading_successor.py::test_r1_*` (five) | At the tag the episode trusts the verdict written with the observation (`answer_exposure_judged`); a row recorded below the tag is judged from persisted rows only, as of its own writing; the answer's own reveals never expose it; a same-second cause display counts as before it. The discriminating observation is written as before | fixed at mvp-0.61 on `algo/mvp-0.61` (`b0b5bc6c9a`); landed with the tag (2026-11-19) |
| T61-2 | mvp-0.61 review, finding 2 (high) | The router's miss-cause sensor and decision-state snapshot read stored posteriors, which still hold an exposed answer recorded below the tag | `test_evidence_reading_successor.py::test_r2_the_router_reads_an_exposed_answer_as_no_update` | `probe_episodes.effective_posterior_as_of`, passed in as a reader (router_objective sits below diagnosis); completed episodes are not reopened | fixed at mvp-0.61 on `algo/mvp-0.61` (`e0abdbe223`); landed with the tag (2026-11-19) |
| T61-3 | mvp-0.61 review, finding 3 (high) | The serving counts (onboarding ceiling, session cap), the value decision's fallback, the baseline projection, the inspector and two audit samples read the stored flags | `test_evidence_reading_successor.py::test_r3_*` (three), `tests/test_probe_observation_admission_readers.py` (the reader guard) | Every reader on the effective view, or listed with why it reads the receipt | fixed at mvp-0.61 on `algo/mvp-0.61` (`88d64cf8a0`); landed with the tag (2026-11-19) |
| T61-4 | mvp-0.61 review, finding 4 (high) | A missing, unrelated or ambiguous run named a replaced self-grade's source (`agent_run(...) or {}` read as `ai`) | `test_evidence_reading_successor.py::test_r4_*` | One existing grading run on the current rows, else the recorded source | fixed at mvp-0.61 on `algo/mvp-0.61` (`c44cee544f`); landed with the tag (2026-11-19) |
| T61-5 | mvp-0.61 review, finding 5 (high) | A same-second legacy regrade read as one grade and bypassed both K5-11 readings | `test_evidence_reading_successor.py::test_r5_*` (two) | `grading_epochs.only_grade`, with an empty-history guard | fixed at mvp-0.61 on `algo/mvp-0.61` (`26ed66fb87`); landed with the tag (2026-11-19) |
| T61-6 | mvp-0.61 review, finding 6 (medium) | The EVSI live report computes a full-weight update for an observation that updates no belief | — | G3-3 | open: G3-3 (not a small change; a report, no tag) |

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |

| E-1 | E1 availability proof | An empty string in `practice_attempts.received_at` is admitted by the SQL cutoff before `created_at`. `known_at` instead falls back to occurrence time and excludes the same row. Migration 244 allows the string and the attempt insert writes every non-null value it receives. Normal exam seeding stamps a timestamp. An in-memory reproduction gives the same disagreement on HEAD and E1. | `tests/test_ro1_availability.py::test_blank_received_at_stores_null_and_agrees_at_cutoffs[empty]`, `tests/test_ro1_availability.py::test_blank_received_at_stores_null_and_agrees_at_cutoffs[whitespace]` | The attempt insert stores NULL for blank receipts. Both cutoff readers then fall back to occurrence time. | fixed on `fix/arc-e-close-rows` |
| E-2 | UX walk harness proof walk (`docs/reviews/ux-walk-harness.md`) | Learner-visible: Today's goal "details" shows "LearnLoop could not confirm whether this action completed" once any attempt with a diagnosis row is newer than a past checkpoint. `get_goal_report_series` replays history in a scratch copy and `goal_series._replay_and_report` prunes `practice_attempts` newer than the checkpoint; `goal_series_store.prune_rows` then deletes the dependent `attempt_diagnoses` rows, whose append-only trigger raises `sqlite3.IntegrityError` (the sidecar maps it to `internal`). Seen on the synthetic established vault after one walk attempt; any vault whose recent attempts were diagnosed should hit it. | `tests/test_goal_series.py::test_series_prunes_append_only_rows_only_in_scratch[diagnosis]`, `tests/test_goal_series.py::test_series_prunes_append_only_rows_only_in_scratch[all-guards]` | Before pruning, goal replay drops the scratch copy's delete guards, read from its schema (`goal_series_store.drop_scratch_delete_guards`); the live database keeps every guard. The test compares trigger definitions and row counts in the live database before and after replay. | fixed on `fix/arc-e-close-rows` |

## From the L4 review: learned models and what their records can reproduce (2026-11-19)

Found by reading, not by a failing test ([refactor-l4-review.md](refactor-l4-review.md)). Each is checked with a test before its fix; the first three change what a rebuild or a fit computes and so wait for an algorithm tag.

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| L4-1 | L4 review (medium) | The episode posterior reads today's item, context, rubric and item parameters for an incidental attempt (`diagnosis/probe_episodes.py`, the incidental-evidence reading and the older classified-outcome fallback), so editing an item's practice mode, difficulty or rubric after the attempt can change an old episode's posterior. Observations with an instrument snapshot are not affected. | new: an incidental attempt, then an edit of the item's rubric denominator: the episode posterior read afterwards equals the one read before | Read the attempt-time values (the attempt's own recorded rubric and the parameters as of the attempt), or snapshot them on the observation as the instrument path does. A tag; with step L4.3 (the episode posterior's core) | open |
| L4-2 | L4 review (medium) | The lesson weight's fit rebuilds the updates between a lesson and its resolving answer with the current `item_irt_params`, while live grading used `resolve_item_irt_params` (the empirical item state included) and the priming offset (`learner/lesson_weight.py`; `attempts/attempts.py`). An intervening update can replay through a different response curve while the fit labels its likelihood `recorded_trajectory`. | Extended F6 reproduces the resolving prior at the recorded mass after a primed repeat, empirical update and authored edits. TE5 also tests missing inputs, upgrade retry and two rebuilds. | Freeze the predecessor curves at upgrade and capture every newly applied grade epoch. The weight and pool fits read the selected epoch and trace each fallback. | Fixed in TE5 part D, mvp-0.70, pending rebase and owner-copy comparison |
| L4-3 | L4 review (low) | A cold forecast predicts through a prior that may come from a covering claim but records only the persisted mastery row (`adaptive_difficulty/forecasters.py`): with no row the receipt says `mastery_state=None`, so the forecast cannot be reproduced after the claim changes. The challenger's history digest has the same limit. | new: a forecast on a topic with a claim-derived prior and no mastery row: the receipt holds the prior used | Record the resolved prior (mean, variance, its source) in the receipt. Receipt shape: check the identity pins. With step L4.1 (claim priors) | open |
| L4-4 | L4 review (low) | The figure prefetch decision's digest leaves out the opening and pause covariances the draw uses (`figures/prefetch_model.py`; `figures/prefetch.py`; `figures/records.py` adds the seed and index only), so a recorded Thompson decision cannot be redrawn from its record once later horizons close. | new: redraw a recorded decision from its record alone and get the same choice | Store the posterior the draw used (or its sufficient statistics) with the decision. With K10.11 (figure records) | open |
| L4-5 | L4 review (note) | The router's rollouts stop on a monotonic deadline between candidates and refinements (`router_objective/rollouts.py`), so equal inputs and seed under different machine load complete different numbers of draws. Not a defect while replay uses the recorded completed count and options instead of rerunning the deadline. | `tests/` already pin draws by default (T3); a replay test that reruns a recorded decision with its recorded count | Keep as a rule of replay; say so in the Router Objective note. With Z1 | open (documentation) |

## From the teacher v2 reviews (2026-11-20)

Found by three independent reviews of the teacher v2 design (spec `docs/specs/spec_teacher_v2.md` §7.4; evidence in `docs/reviews/teacher-v2-arc-reviews-2026-11-20.md`). The fix is a router successor in seven steps; steps 1 to 4 change nothing served or stored ([algorithm-check-credit-steps-1-4.md](algorithm-check-credit-steps-1-4.md)).

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| TV2-1 | Three independent reviews of the teacher v2 design; the spec's §7.4 | Lesson offers are credited with delayed checks that never happen. When the router values a lesson offer, the rollout applies the offer's committed delayed check as a full practice dose (mass 1.0) at the first day boundary at or past its date, before testing whether the simulated learner returns, with no minutes charged (`router_objective/rollouts.py:844-849`); the offer builder always attaches one retention check whether or not any question exists (`daily_plan/lesson_offer.py:352-354`); the eight outcome columns for commitments wrap at the ninth (`rollouts.py:153`, `:848`). On the design's worked fixture the accepted branch is worth 0.600000000 and should be worth 0.500000000; the mixed offer 0.524744871 against 0.474744871. | `tests/test_check_credit_characterization.py`: today's pricing pinned; `test_both_paths_a_check_happens_only_on_a_simulated_return`, `test_both_paths_a_check_is_charged_its_minutes_on_the_session_clock`, `test_both_paths_the_ninth_commitment_draws_its_own_outcome` (strict xfails at step 1, both-paths tests from step 4); `test_intended_an_offer_with_no_eligible_question_credits_no_check` (strict xfail until the activation; at the activation the both-tags `test_both_tags_an_offer_with_no_eligible_question`) | Seven steps: 1 to 4 on `algo/check-credit` (characterization; the pure as-of receipt statistics with the one-trial seed; the availability predicate shared with `delayed_check_words` and the dry minutes estimate; the new rollout path, unreachable), then a read-only stored-row report on copies, the activation at `mvp-0.62`, and the record with the comparison on copies of the owner's vaults | steps 1 to 5 landed (Nov 20: the characterization, the receipt statistics, the adapters, the unreachable new path, the stored-row report); steps 6 and 7 built on `algo/check-credit-activate`: the activation at `mvp-0.62` for new decisions and its record ([algorithm-mvp-0.62.md](algorithm-mvp-0.62.md)); the owner-copy comparison is the orchestrator's |
| TV2-2 | The check-credit activation's explanation of the owner-copy report (TV2-1's step 6) | An unpractised topic never forgets in the router's memory model, and its first graded attempt switches it onto forgetting. The combiner reads an unpractised topic as `σ(a0 + aA·η)` (AD's predictive, constant in time) and a practised one as `σ(a0 + aA·η + aR·(logit R − η))` with FSRS's `R` decaying from the attempt's first-review stability (`router_objective/memory.py::perform`, `rollouts.py::_practice`). So the first practice of a topic AD rates well can lower its forecast below never practising it: one topic with `η = 2`, the combiner at `(0, 1, 0.5)`, a lesson and one more practice at day 4 with no return in a 30-day window is worth 3.917 less with the practice than without (`tail_reward` −1.179, `terminal_reward` −2.738), under either review draw. This is what made the phantom check of TV2-1 count against lessons on the owner's copy. It is in the rollouts, not in the check credit: every first practice is priced this way (a tail session's, a new-practice block's, a repair's), and from `mvp-0.62` a delivered check's too; an undelivered check no longer triggers it. | `tests/test_check_credit_characterization.py::test_finding_a_first_graded_attempt_switches_an_unpractised_topic_onto_forgetting` (characterization of the old and new paths, passing: it pins the effect, not the fix) | A memory-model successor: how a topic's first attempt should start its forgetting (for instance FSRS's first-review stability read against AD's predictive, so a first success never lowers the forecast below the unpractised one), owner's call | open; deadline: before the teacher v2 arc closes (it distorts every lesson's and first practice's value); not in `mvp-0.62` |
| TV2-3 | The same explanation | The stored-row report's current-copy scenario (`scripts/check_credit_report.py::_current_pair`, TV2-1's step 5) evaluated both pricings with no rollout options, so it ran the per-block tail and the review draw from AD's predictive alone (mvp-0.49's), not the memory model's own draw every decision has served since `mvp-0.50`. Under that draw a phantom check on a practised topic AD rates low is mostly a lapse whatever FSRS's `R` says, and the lapse cuts its stability: one practised topic (stability 30, `η = −2`) is worth 8.163 more without the phantom under the old draw, and 0.164 less under the served one. The owner-copy numbers of the step-5 report (+0.018 at 8 draws, +0.065 at 64) were computed under the old draw. | `tests/test_check_credit_report.py::test_current_copy_from_real_builders_is_read_only` (the scenario's options; failed before with `KeyError: 'rollout_options'`); `tests/test_check_credit_characterization.py::test_finding_under_the_mvp_0_49_draw_a_phantom_lapse_collapses_a_practised_topics_memory` | The scenario runs under `objective_chooser.rollout_options(config)` of the copy and the report records them | fixed on `algo/check-credit-activate` (the orchestrator reruns the report on the owner's copy) |

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| D8-1 | The rebuild-equivalence review's F2 (2026-10-25), pinned as a strict xfail until D8 | Two grades of one learning object applied within one second, in the opposite order from their ids (an id is minted when grading starts, `created_at` when the grade is applied, in whole seconds), replay in `(created_at, id)` order: live applied them in transaction order, so a rebuild left other mastery, and each replayed attempt saw another recent-attempt window and canonical prefix than its live write had. | `test_rebuild_equivalence_review.py::test_two_same_second_attempts_committed_in_reverse_id_order_rebuild_to_live` (the strict xfail, marker removed) and `tests/test_same_second_order.py` (boundaries, two connections, rollback and retry, regrade, legacy and new rows in one second, the other readers) | Successor mvp-0.65 (`docs/reviews/algorithm-mvp-0.65.md`): `apply_attempt` reserves an application sequence inside its write transaction (`attempt_application_order`, migration 261) and every prefix reader orders by `(created_at, sequence, id)` through `learnloop.db.attempt_order`; the upgrade ranks earlier attempts in the predecessor's order (`legacy_order_unknown`) and rebuilds. Historical ties keep the predecessor's reading: their real order was never recorded | fixed at mvp-0.65 (the landing of `refactor/d8-same-second-order`) |
| D8-2 | Followups Oct 27 (3), found by the mobile PR-3/PR-4 landing on an owner copy and pinned as three strict xfails | A grade's relative coverage and its legacy breadth denominator count the facets of its learning object's active items (`required_facets`). A sibling closed outside the attempt ledger (retired, parked for review, superseded, removed from the vault, closed directly) has no time replay can apply the closure at: replay resets attempted items, leaves an unattempted sibling's current flag in place and restores inactivity only after the learning object's last attempt, so an unattempted retired sibling looked closed throughout history and an attempted one open throughout; the rebuild left other mastery and `due_at` than live. | `tests/test_rebuild_closed_row_answer.py` (the three strict xfails, marker removed; capture and both readings, attempted sibling, close and reopen, removal from the vault, a close in the same second between answers, regrade, rollback, missing capture, upgrade, canonical order) | Successor mvp-0.66 (`docs/reviews/algorithm-mvp-0.66.md`): the live grade application reads the set once, counts it for coverage and breadth alike in sorted order and records it with the attempt (`attempt_lifecycle_inputs`, migration 263); replay and regrade read it back, and an attempt applied at the tag without it refuses to replay. The upgrade records, for every earlier attempt, the set the predecessor's own replay reads at it (`legacy_reconstructed`), then rebuilds. Earlier closures keep the predecessor's reading: when they happened was never recorded | fixed at mvp-0.66 (the landing of `refactor/d8-retired-items`) |
| D8-3 | Found by the kept-replaced-claims stage (mvp-0.36) and pinned as a parametrized strict xfail at mvp-0.35 and mvp-0.36 | A claim that never re-anchors (a changed starting level from the profile setter, a manual claim) made after a learning object's mastery row was materialized leaves the live row as it was, by the profile setter's explicit contract, while replay seeded a practised learning object from the claims made before its first attempt and an attempt-free one from the claims in force at rebuild time: a rebuild after such a claim left other mastery than live, for practised and unpractised topics alike. | `tests/test_learner_report_superseded.py` (the strict xfail at mvp-0.35/0.36 kept as below-tag characterization; the equality scenario on a born-successor vault and on an mvp-0.66 vault upgraded before it) and `tests/test_mastery_seed_replay.py` (the profile contract at both tags, what is recorded, interrupted sync, attempt and re-anchor materializations, rollback, manual claims, several replacements, a re-anchor before the first attempt, attempt-free replay, the rebuild materializing an unsynced learning object, the upgrade, goldens) | Successor mvp-0.67 (`docs/reviews/algorithm-mvp-0.67.md`): the writer that first persists a learning object's mastery records every claim its seed considered in the same transaction (`mastery_seed_inputs`, migration 265); replay and the attempt-free rebuild seed from the record and apply only the re-anchoring claims made after it. The upgrade records, for every learning object, the seed the predecessor's own rebuild gives it (`legacy_reconstructed`), then rebuilds. | RD0-5 | The review's 5 | A vault already at the latest tag when migration 262 arrived got empty projections and no whole-vault rebuild, so its history was never projected until an explicit rebuild. | `test_reader_rd0_spans.py::test_opening_a_vault_already_at_the_latest_tag_projects_its_history_once` | Receipt-guarded one-time initialization on open (`passage_spans.initialize_on_open`, receipts in `projection_initializations`, migration 264), independent of the algorithm tag | fixed |
