## From the Reader workbench v2 context menu frontend (RD5b, 2026-11-23)

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RD5b-1 | RD5b, the layout carried from RD4's frontend round | A pad stack's window was titled "notes", and its ⓘ sat as "ⓘ yours" in a bar above the pads; the mockup's title bar reads "Scratchpads ⓘ yours" with the ⓘ circled after the title | `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx > a pad stack's title bar reads Scratchpads ⓘ yours…` (fails with the old title) | The title bar reads "Scratchpads", then the circled ⓘ (17 px, 1 px `#3a3a3a`, `#8b8b9d`, as measured) opening "where your words go" under it, then "yours" as the window's place; the stack's own bar shows only where no desk holds it (a stack mounted alone) | fixed |
| RD5b-2 | RD5b, the vitest run | The DeskShell suite's pads fake answered `passage.union_range` with no `spans`: the passage marks' read threw inside its promise, six unhandled rejections, and vitest exits 1 on the suite (also on RD4's frontend fix round's tip) | `npx vitest run src/screens/desks/DeskShell.test.tsx` (exit 1, "Errors 6 errors") | The fake answers in the contract's shape (`spans: []`) | fixed |

## From the Reader workbench v2 menus review (RD5, Codex Astra, 2026-11-23; backend half of one fix round)

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RD5-1 | The review's blocker 1 | A menu opened on exam or held-out material (a passage, a cue, a highlight, a pad line linked there) read the passage's raw words and its job sent them to search (a paid query embedding with retrieval on) and to the verification judge; the elsewhere search's scope also held protected passages, so one could be judged and promoted. | `tests/test_menu_protected_material.py::test_a_menu_on_held_out_material_sends_its_words_to_no_search_and_no_judge`, `::test_search_elsewhere_never_finds_or_judges_protected_material`, `::test_a_whole_exam_reading_is_protected_the_same_way`: failed on the landed code (the held-out passage's words were the query and the judge's question; the scope allowed the held-out passage) | The one predicate (`exam_withheld`, as the union read and `passage.content` use it) asked of the object's passages, through pad lineage, before any words are used: protected, the words are empty, nothing is searched or judged, and the reply says `withheld`; the search scope leaves exam material out and a hit in it is dropped before the judge | fixed |
| RD5-2 | The review's blocker 2 | A passage held out after the cue judge linked it still gave promoted "next step" rows and new gutter proposals with its target and stored quote, and a stored proposal was returned without asking protection again; a link from a protected passage was followed too. | `::test_a_passage_held_out_after_it_was_linked_yields_no_row_and_no_proposal`, `::test_a_held_out_passage_promotes_no_passage_elsewhere`: failed on the landed code (the stored proposal and its quote shown; a next-step row with its quote) | Passage links are filtered at read time, origins and targets; `pad.proposals` asks the predicate of each stored proposal's target and its block's passages at every read and leaves a protected one out whole; a block about protected material gets no search proposal; without the vault nothing is proposed (fail closed) | fixed |
| RD5-3 | The review's major on retried menu requests (server side) | Every `passage.promoted` minted a new job: a retry after a lost reply ran the search (and any rewrite or judge request) again. | `tests/test_menu_promoted_rows.py::test_a_retried_menu_request_answers_with_its_job_and_runs_once`: failed on the landed code (no request identity) | An optional `request_id`, vault-scoped in the polled-jobs registry: a repeat answers with the first request's job (its work runs once), the same id for another object is refused (`request_reused`) | fixed (server; the client sends it) |
| RD5-4 | The review's major on interrupted jobs | A polled job interrupted by a vault switch whose work finished afterwards (a request already sent) read as `done` with its rows when the vault was opened again, against the module's own promise. | `::test_a_menu_whose_vault_was_switched_away_from_delivers_nothing`: failed on the landed code (status done, rows delivered) | An interrupted job that finishes publishes no result and reads as failed (`interrupted`); a context close keeps a sent request's outcome as before | fixed |
| RD5-5 | The review's major on "save to my queue" (server side) | `author_practice_item` had no durable identity outside a capture: a double click or a retry after a lost reply made a second card. | `tests/test_sidecar_item_authoring.py::test_a_retried_authoring_request_saves_one_item`: failed on the landed code (the key was refused; no identity) | An optional `client_idempotency_key`: the card's id is derived from it, so a repeat returns the first card (no second card, no second provenance event); kept out of the capture route's request hash | fixed (server; the client sends it) |
| RD5-6 | The review's minor on What this knows (server side) | `passage.knows` never handed the linked notes to the union read, so its notes were always empty where `passage.union_range` showed them. | `::test_what_this_knows_carries_the_notes_linked_beside_the_passage`: failed on the landed code | One adapter hands the notes to every union read in the module | fixed (server; the panel's groups are the client's) |
| RD5-7 | The review's minor on duplicate candidates | A promoted row reached from two of a pad line's passages kept the first one's learner time, so its rank depended on passage order. | `::test_a_row_reached_from_two_passages_ranks_by_its_latest_act`: failed on the landed code | The row keeps the latest learner act among its occurrences | fixed |
| RD5-8 | The review's major on Make it practice (server side) | `reader.import_exercise` writes schedulable items when its job lands; nothing lets the learner see and accept the draft before admission, as the spec's review sheet asks. | `ExerciseSheet.test.tsx > shows the exercise's own words, imports only when asked, then lists what it made…` (client, RD5-50) | Stopped: a draft held before admission needs persisted state (a new import status or table) and an admit RPC; options in `docs/reviews/reader-rd5a.md` §9 The client does option (A) now: the sheet shows the exercise's own words before the import and, once it lands, lists the items it made with edit and retire, said to be in the queue already. Draft-then-admit (B) stays an owner option: it needs storage of its own | fixed by (A); (B) an owner option |

## From the Reader workbench v2 pen and ink (RD8a, 2026-11-23)

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RD8a-1 | RD8a's frontend worktree, merging RD3's fix round onto the RD8a backend | `ink.transcribe`'s handler kept its own per-vault map of polled jobs and called `PolledJobs.get/cancel/receipt` with an id alone; RD3's fix round keyed every polled job by vault (RD3-1), so with both landed every reading RPC raised `TypeError` and a vault switch would not have interrupted a reading. | `tests/test_reader_rd8a_rpcs.py` (5 failed after the merge: `PolledJobs.get() missing 1 required positional argument`); `::test_a_reading_belongs_to_its_vault` fails with the poll's vault taken from the job instead of the open vault | The reading jobs use `polled_jobs.registry(ctx, "ink_transcribe_jobs")` and `vault_key(ctx)` on every start, poll and cancel (so `interrupt_vault` reaches them); which job reads which strokes is kept per vault on the context | fixed |
| RD8a-2 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 2) | A reading that finished after the line's strokes changed (and after a newer reading was written) became the block's words and was emitted: the apply compared the head's reading with the row, never the strokes standing now; and the client marked the line read whatever was written during the request. | `tests/test_reader_rd8a_fix.py::test_a_reading_of_strokes_that_changed_meanwhile_is_kept_but_never_replaces_the_newer_one`; `pen/inkCache.test.ts` "a reading of strokes written to meanwhile leaves the line unread" and "a reading the server set aside …" | `pads.ink.reading_decision` (one rule for the apply, a cached answer and `ink.list`): a reading becomes the block's words only while its hash is the standing strokes' hash, inside the reading's transaction; otherwise it is kept and answered `strokes_changed`. The ink cache counts each line's local changes and leaves a line unread when its reading started at another count or the server says `strokes_changed` | fixed |
| RD8a-3 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 3) | Erasing every stroke of an ink line left LearnLoop's reading of them as the block's words, still emitted as learner text. | `tests/test_reader_rd8a_fix.py::test_erasing_handwriting_takes_its_automatic_transcription_and_what_was_read_from_it` (and `…keeps_the_learners_own_words`, `…partial_erase_leaves_the_words…`); `pen/inkPad.test.tsx` "erasing every stroke of a line takes LearnLoop's reading of them too" | The `erase_ink` pad op, when it leaves no standing stroke and the head is an automatic transcription, writes a blank successor (the reading's payload key dropped; client-minted `version_id`, mirrored in the client's pad model), so emission supersedes what was read from it. A learner correction is never touched; after a partial erase the words stay until the line is read again | fixed |
| RD8a-4 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 4) | Undoing a mark while its annotation was still being made removed only the display: the annotation was made and its ink saved afterwards; a failed delete of a known annotation was swallowed. | `pen/PenLayer.test.tsx` "undo while the annotation is being made removes it once made …" and "a removal that fails puts the mark back …" | A mark taken back while saving is remembered; when the capture answers, its annotation is deleted and its ink never saved. A failed delete puts the mark back with "couldn't take this mark back · undo to try again", and undo retries | fixed |
| RD8a-5 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 10) | A reopened source re-recognized saved marks from scroller pixels on today's layout, so at another width a disagreement could be drawn over other words than the ones it belongs to. | `pen/PenLayer.test.tsx` "a mark is drawn from its annotation's anchor …" and "a mark whose anchor can no longer be vouched for paints no words" | Saved marks are drawn from the annotation's anchor (the sidecar's placement on the open reading, else its segments; nothing where it is flagged, elsewhere or needs re-anchoring), the words found by their quote in the passage's text; the kind comes from the annotation (a bracket told by its ink's shape). Its ink stays where written while that still marks the same words, else it is moved beside them | fixed |
| RD8a-6 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 5) | A margin note's reading arriving while the learner typed replaced their draft. | `pen/PenLayer.test.tsx` "a reading arriving while the learner types is offered beside the draft …" | The note's editor is marked dirty on input; a reading arriving then is offered beside the draft ("LearnLoop reads: … · use it"), never written over it | fixed |
| RD8a-7 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 8) | Cancel then retry of the same strokes could pay twice: the cancelled job no longer reserved the strokes while its request was still out. | `tests/test_reader_rd8a_fix_rpcs.py::test_a_retry_after_a_cancel_waits_for_the_cancelled_request_instead_of_paying_again` | A cancelled job keeps the reservation until its thread ends; a retry starts a job that waits for it (stopping if itself cancelled or its context closes) and then answers from the kept reading | fixed |
| RD8a-8 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 12) | A reading cancelled (or its window closed) before `ink.transcribe` answered was followed anyway: the late job id was polled and never cancelled; a source note's job was never cancelled on undo or close. | `pen/inkCache.test.ts` "a reading cancelled before its start answered …"; `pen/PenLayer.test.tsx` "a note taken back before its reading's start answered …" and "closing the window stops a note's reading on the server" | Each line's reading carries an attempt number checked after every await; a late start of a stale attempt is cancelled on the server and never registered. A margin note keeps its job id; undo and the window's close stop its poll and cancel the job | fixed |
| RD8a-9 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 9) | A reading that finished after a vault switch or shutdown woke the context's post-attempt worker, restarting a stopped worker or starting the next vault's. | `tests/test_reader_rd8a_fix_rpcs.py::test_a_reading_finishing_after_its_context_moved_on_keeps_its_facts_but_wakes_no_worker` | `_after_reading` takes the job's `live`: the facts are still written in the reading's own vault, the worker is woken only while the job's epoch is open | fixed |
| RD8a-10 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 6) | Lifting an ink line out left its strokes on the stub: the lifted block showed no handwriting, and reading it again would have paid. | `tests/test_reader_rd8a_fix.py::test_lifting_an_ink_line_out_keeps_its_handwriting_and_its_reading`; `pen/inkCache.test.ts` "a lifted line takes its strokes and reading …" | `lift_out` of an ink block saves the standing strokes again for the lifted block (same stroke ids and points, so the same hash) and copies the reading of exactly those strokes, so nothing is read again; the client moves the line at once and reads the new pad once the lift is answered | fixed |
| RD8a-11 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 13) | `refreshPad` could lay an older pad snapshot over a newer local correction acknowledged while the read was out. | `store.test.ts` "a pad read that started before a correction … never lays the older pad over it" | The store counts each pad's local changes; a read whose pad changed since it started is dropped | fixed |
| RD8a-12 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 14) | An erase the server refused stayed erased on screen: the ink cache loaded each pad once and nothing read it again. | `pen/inkCache.test.ts` "an erase the server refused shows the strokes it still holds …" | After an erase is answered the cache reads the line's pad again (`reconcile`); a pad shown again is read again too; strokes written here and not yet acknowledged stay on top | fixed |
| RD8a-13 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 7) | Unsent ink was said to be "kept on this device", but the outbox lives in memory and is lost when the app closes. | `pen/inkCache.test.ts` "unsent ink is said to be kept while the app stays open …" | The words say what is true: "your ink isn't sent yet · it is kept while LearnLoop stays open". A durable outbox is the RD-spike's storage choice, left to the owner | fixed (wording) |
| RD8a-14 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 1) | `ink_transcriptions`' replacing-INSERT guard checked the primary key alone: an `INSERT OR REPLACE` with a fresh id and an existing owner and strokes deleted the standing reading without the delete guard firing. | `tests/test_ink_transcriptions_guard_migration.py::test_neither_key_can_be_met_again` | Migration 269 recreates the guard to refuse an insert meeting either key; fixture upgraded in its own commit | fixed |
| RD8a-15 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 15) | An underline or strike ending inside a word anchored half of it. | `pen/PenLayer.test.tsx` "an underline becomes a highlight snapped to the words …" (its assertion pinned the half-words) | `rangeBetween` widens both ends to whole words within their text | fixed |
| RD8a-16 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 16) | The reading offered beside the learner's correction did not survive a retry (a cached answer said `reason: null`) or a reload (`ink.list` carried no reason, the cache built no offer). | `tests/test_reader_rd8a_fix.py::test_a_cached_reading_beside_the_learners_correction_keeps_its_reason`, `tests/test_reader_rd8a_fix_rpcs.py::test_the_listed_reading_beside_the_learners_correction_says_why_it_is_not_the_words`; `pen/inkCache.test.ts` "a reading kept beside the learner's correction is offered again after a reload" | Cached answers and `ink.list`'s transcription carry `applied` and `reason` from the same rule; the cache offers a kept reading again on load | fixed |
| RD8a-17 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 17) | Pausing a pad between the pen's down and up kept the stroke under way, saved on the pointer-up after the window resumed. | `pen/inkPad.test.tsx` "a pause between the pen's down and up drops the stroke under way" | `InkSurface` cancels its input and clears the live stroke when it pauses | fixed |
| RD8a-18 | RD8a's fix round, writing the erase-everything test | One barrel gesture over an ink line sent `erase_ink` for the same stroke again on each move before the line redrew; the server refuses an erase naming a stroke that no longer stands, and the refusal takes the whole op. | `pen/inkPad.test.tsx` "erasing every stroke of a line …" (8 erase ops for 4 strokes) | `InkSurface` erases each stroke once a gesture (as the source layer already did) | fixed |
| RD8a-19 | RD8a's adversarial review (finding 11) | Each margin stroke made its own interpretation annotation and started its own reading, so a two-stroke "x" was two notes and neither reading saw the whole character. | `pen/PenLayer.test.tsx` "a two-stroke x is one note …" and "two notes beside different passages stay two" (the stroke-per-note code made two captures and two readings, and captured before the note ended) | The orchestrator's default, option (a): a margin stroke beside the open note's passage, overlapping its strokes grown by one rendered line (read from the page), joins it; anything else ends it; one annotation and one reading per note, when it ends | fixed |
| RD8a-20 | The owner's decision on reading handwriting (2026-11-23) | Handwriting was read by a vision LLM on the plan route, which today reads no images (every reading refused with `cannot_read_images`); the owner decided GLM-OCR reads it and its text is the reading directly. | `tests/test_reader_ink_glm_ocr.py` (10, each failing on the vision route); `pen/inkCache.test.ts` "handwriting text recognition cannot read says why …" | `ink.transcribe` sends the strokes' image to GLM-OCR through the extractor's one entry for an image (`recognise_image`), admitted as ingest admits a page image; the recognised text is kept unchanged; the vision route, its paid task, price setting, disclosure and config field are removed (the field retired) | fixed |

| RD6-1 | RD6's adversarial review (blocker) | While a cold check was due on an LO, the band's path still named it: `_held_line` blanked only the reason and alternates' differences, so the LO's title and phrase, its section titles (a heading "Eigenvalues" names it), treatment words and the exercise label reached the path view, a band dot's primary, `next`, and "you are reading" (the window's own place words) | `test_reader_rd6_fix_protection.py::test_a_held_los_own_path_row_names_nothing`, the field table (`test_every_text_bearing_band_field_is_masked`), `goal/protection.test.tsx` | One server function masks every text-bearing field of the band, its path and the lens (`curriculum.goal_lens`, `protect_band`/`protect_lens`), required by both reply builders; the "you are reading" line takes the server's neutral title for a held section | fixed |
| RD6-2 | RD6's adversarial review (blocker) | The masking held only the goal's own LOs: a section covering X (check due) and Y showed Y's goal X's section title, why and callout, a reason written for Y could name X, and another goal containing only Y dropped X before `held` was computed | `test_reader_rd6_fix_protection.py::test_another_goals_due_check_masks_a_shared_section_and_names_nothing_of_it`, `test_every_text_bearing_lens_field_is_masked` | The protection is computed from every due LO whatever the goal (`goal_lens_reads.protection`): the sections treating them (coverage, placements in any goal, check hosts), the LOs depending on them (prerequisites, assumed in a section read for them), and their titles as whole words in any text | fixed |
| RD6-3 | RD6's adversarial review (major) | Stale coverage could make needed text skippable: a needed LO's rows on a section not stored as a placement were dropped once the section changed (section hash), the LO stayed "judged" by its statement, and the section's only current row (another LO's, or "not about") made it skippable and dimmed | `test_reader_rd6_fix_freshness.py::test_a_section_changed_since_a_needed_los_rows_is_unknown_for_it_never_skippable` | A goal LO whose rows on a section were all written against an earlier version of it, with none standing now, leaves that section partial (unsettled), never skippable or unmapped (`SectionReads.stale`) | fixed |
| RD6-4 | RD6's adversarial review (major) | "Tried once, found nowhere" stayed settled after the library changed: the attempt was keyed by the LO's statement alone, so a reading added later was called skippable without the LO being tried against it (and a restart turned the same data back to partial) | `test_reader_rd6_fix_freshness.py::test_an_lo_tried_and_found_nowhere_is_settled_only_against_that_library` | An attempt is keyed by the statement and the library's scope signature (`DP.attempt_keys`): once a reading is added, re-extracted or switched, the LO is unsettled for the lens and owed again for the coverage | fixed |
| RD6-5 | RD6's adversarial review (major) | `goal.band` and `goal.lens` entered `curriculum._require`, which finishes a publication a stopped process left half done: a read could rewrite `profile/goals.yaml`, delete journal files and take the vault lock (RD6a's no-write test counted table rows only) | `test_reader_rd6_fix_reads.py::test_band_and_lens_reads_leave_an_unfinished_goal_change_for_the_next_action` (every vault file's size and time) | The two reads take `curriculum._require_read` (the same refusal, no recovery); the next curriculum action finishes the publication | fixed |
| RD6-6 | RD6's adversarial review (major) | An old lens reply overwrote a current one: a reply asked under goal A, landing after goal B's, installed A's levels under B's band (and one asked before a depth change or "read it anyway" reversed it); an open why card kept its old section across refreshes and a goal change | `goal/freshness.test.tsx` ("drops a lens reply asked under another goal…", "keeps an open why card…") | Each lens request carries a ticket: a reply lands only while it is the latest asked for its reading under the goal shown; the why card is re-read from the latest lens (closed when its section is gone) and closed on a goal change | fixed |
| RD6-7 | RD6's adversarial review (major) | A ready band never learned of a change: no read after a check fell due (its names and reasons stayed shown), none after an answer, coverage or goal change elsewhere, the goals list fetched once (a goal paused elsewhere stayed the active one), and a reading coming back into view kept its old levels | `goal/freshness.test.tsx` ("reads the goals, the band and the readings again…", "reads the band again when the next check falls due", "asks again for a reading that comes back into view"), `test_reader_rd6_fix_reads.py::test_the_band_says_when_the_next_check_falls_due_so_it_is_read_again_then` | `goal.band` says when the next check falls due (`next_check_due`, from the serving view the due checks already build) and the band is read again then; the app's own writes (the query cache's goals, queue, sources, graph and vault tags) read the goals, band and readings again; a reading leaving view is asked again on return | fixed |
| RD6-8 | RD6's adversarial review (major) | Coverage finishing did not start the path judgment: the open's one `curriculum.path` started coverage only, the later reads are read-only `goal.band`, and a "pending" path was polled every six seconds for ever | `goal/freshness.test.tsx` ("starts the judgment a path owes once coverage is done…") | A band whose path owes a judgment (pending or stale) with no coverage running starts it with `curriculum.path` once per path state; only running work (judging, covering) is polled | fixed |
| RD6-9 | RD6's adversarial review (major) | A transcript paragraph took its first cue's section: one beginning in skippable S1 and running into core S2 was dimmed and marked skippable throughout | `goal/watch.test.tsx` ("gives a passage group crossing into a needed section that section's mark…") | A group shows its most needed section (`mostNeededSpan`: undimmed first, then by level; a cue no section maps counts as unmapped) | fixed |
| RD6-10 | RD6's adversarial review (major) | Source summaries overclaimed "not needed": a shelf row with one skippable and one unmapped section read "not needed for your goal"; a video head named only its first range on the path and called "the rest" not needed whatever the rest's mapping; without a path range one skippable section labelled a partly unmapped video not needed | `goal/shelf.test.tsx` ("calls a source not needed only when every section is known not needed"), `goal/watch.test.tsx` ("names every range on the path…") | A whole-source or "the rest" claim needs every section concerned mapped and skippable, later or already shown (`noneNeeded`); otherwise the row reads "partly mapped" and the head makes no claim; the head names every range on the path (touching ranges as one) | fixed |
| RD6-11 | RD6's adversarial review (minor) | The passage being read was not always exempt from dimming: in a book the exemption used the active passage only, not the one scrolled to (`readingSpan`); in a video only an annotated cue kept full colour, so the playing cue's colour lost to the dimming rule | `goal/components.test.tsx` ("the reading passage…"), `goal/watch.test.tsx` ("keeps an annotated cue, and the cue playing…") | The Reader passes `reading` to the lens, which keeps it at full colour; the playing cue gets `gl-keep` | fixed |
| RD6-12 | RD6's adversarial review (minor) | A paused pad still asked the lens: every loaded pad's anchors went into the goal lens's readings past the paused-reading filter, so a refresh asked for a reading no active window needed | `goal/components.test.tsx` ("a pad's anchors…") | A pad anchor carries `paused` (no unpaused window shows its pad; computed in DeskShell from the windows' stacks and lifecycles), and a paused anchor's reading is not asked; its cached levels stay | fixed |
| RD6-13 | RD6's adversarial review (minor) | The path view said "change: type what's missing or not needed" on a plain span with no input or action | `goal/components.test.tsx` ("takes the learner's correction of the goal's scope…") | The line opens an input whose words go to the goal as a scope correction through `curriculum.set_scope` (kind `correction`, the RPC that owns it); without a handler the line is not offered | fixed |
| RD6-14 | RD6's adversarial review (minor) | The companion's "◎ goal in context" badge said the tutor answers with the goal and its LOs in mind; the desk context is only recorded with a question, never sent to the tutor's prompt (and for the default goal the recorded desk goal is null) | `companion/Companion.test.tsx` ("the context line … says the goal is in view, never that the tutor answers with it") | The badge reads "◎ goal in view" and its title says the tutor's answers don't take the goal into account yet (sending it is a prompt change, its own step) | fixed |
| RD10-1 | RD10's adversarial review (blocker) | Follow-along and the chapters RPCs named a topic whose cold check is due: an accepted chapter's title, the transcript section's label, LO tags and correspondent passages' titles reached the chip (linked or unlinked), the chapters panel and the scrub track's ticks; neither path consulted due checks, and a cached answer outlived a check falling due | `test_reader_rd10_fix_protection.py` (field tables over `protect_follow` and `protect_chapters`, every string of the four replies walked end to end, `next_check_due`), `video/protection.test.tsx` (DOM: text, titles, attributes; an answer from before a check fell due) | The goal lens's one protection (`curriculum.goal_lens`: `protect_follow`, `protect_chapters`, the same due-LO set through `handlers.goal_lens.protection_now` and the same neutral words) masks `reader.follow_along`, `chapters.list`, `chapters.decide` and a suggestion's result at every poll; the replies carry `next_check_due`, and the desk drops and re-asks an answer past it, the chapters panel reads again then | fixed |
| RD10-2 | RD10's adversarial review (major) | A suggestion cancelled after its request was sent still stored its chapters (the final guard checked protection, not the job's cancellation or epoch), while the poll said cancelled and the panel "nothing was kept"; a closed epoch or a vault switch and back likewise stored | `test_reader_rd10_fix_protection.py::test_a_suggestion_cancelled_while_its_request_is_out_stores_nothing` | `VC.store_suggestions(wanted=...)` checks, inside the write transaction, that the job is not cancelled and its context's epoch is open (`not job.cancelled and job.live()`); otherwise nothing is stored and the job ends cancelled, so "nothing was kept" is true | fixed |
| RD10-3 | RD10's adversarial review (major) | The chapters judge read the curriculum switch once, from the captured vault: switched off and reloaded meanwhile, a request or retry was still sent and its chapters stored | `test_reader_rd10_fix_protection.py::test_the_curriculum_switched_off_while_a_suggestion_runs_stores_nothing` | The judgment's `CoverageGuard` takes `gate=SC.enabled`, read from the vault as it is at every request and at the write; closed, the suggestion is `unavailable` (`curriculum_disabled`) and nothing is stored | fixed |
| RD10-4 | RD10's adversarial review (major) | The last-playing video was one value for every desk: a video played on desk B made desk A fall back to its first video, so `alt+;` stamped the wrong clock and anchor (or refused) and follow-along followed the wrong driver | `video/video.test.tsx` ("the driver per desk…") | Each video window's last play is kept as a sequence; the driver is the latest among the current desk's videos | fixed |
| RD10-5 | RD10's adversarial review (major) | A chapter decision's reply was merged into the list it was asked from: a second reply restored the first chapter's old state (resurrecting a dismissed chapter, dropping an accepted tick); a decision refused as decided elsewhere only showed an error, and two windows on one video never reconciled | `video/video.test.tsx` ("chapter decisions", 2) | Replies merge into the chapters shown now; a refused decision reads the stored chapters again; a stored decision is counted on the desk (`VideoDesk.decided`), and every window showing the video reads its chapters again | fixed |
| RD10-6 | RD10's adversarial review (minor) | A follow answer applied whatever had changed while it was asked: a book gone offscreen, showing another reading, or another video driving still took the old answer and scrolled | `video/video.test.tsx` ("a follow answer that comes back late…") | The answer is applied only while its book is on the desk, not paused, on the same reading, and its video still drives; otherwise it is dropped and the refresh asks again where still wanted | fixed |
| RD10-7 | RD10's adversarial review (minor) | A question marked let go (or fruitful) kept resurfacing when the refresh after the mark failed part-way: the previous lists stayed authoritative | `DeskShell.test.tsx` ("a question let go leaves the shelf at once, even when the refresh after it fails") | A successful closing mark takes the question out of the shelf's list and the all-carried list at once, and no later refresh restores it this session | fixed |
| RD10-8 | RD10's adversarial review (minor) | One carried question beside two passages of one window made two marks with one key: a click opened both popovers and React's keys collided | `pads/passageMarks.test.tsx` | A carried mark's key includes the passage it sits beside (`surfaced` stays once per question) | fixed |
| RD10-9 | RD10's adversarial review (minor) | A video judged before could not be answered from the ledger once today's budget was spent: admission returned `capped` before the ledger lookup | `test_reader_rd10_fix_protection.py::test_a_judged_video_replays_from_the_ledger_with_the_budget_spent_and_an_unjudged_one_is_capped` | A spent budget no longer refuses at admission: the ledger answers a judged video, and the daily envelope refuses the paid request itself when one is needed (`capped`, nothing sent) | fixed |
| RD10-10 | RD10's adversarial review (minor) | Migration 268's once-only decision has a replacement hole: `INSERT OR REPLACE` with a decided chapter's id deletes it without firing `video_chapters_keep_decided` (recursive triggers are off). | RD10-11 | RD10's fix round, the D15 sweep (owner of the code: RD9a) | A lesson window's tab and the path view's lesson row named the LO a due check covers (`lesson.list`'s title is the LO's title, its goal the stored goal text), and the first note under such a lesson stored that title in its additions pad's title | `test_reader_rd10_fix_sweep.py::test_a_lesson_on_a_topic_whose_check_is_due_names_nothing_in_its_tab`, `lesson/lessonWindows.test.tsx` ("a lesson held for a due check gives its additions pad no topic…") | `lesson.list` masks through the same protection (`GL.protect_lessons`: title neutral, goal withheld, `held`); a held lesson's additions pad is created titled "your additions" | fixed |
| RD10-12 | RD10's fix round, the D15 sweep (owner: RD7, inline checks and veils) | An open lesson's own text (`lesson.view`: its turns, checks, close card) teaches the LO a due check covers, and the lesson window shows it in full | none | Owner decision with RD7's veils (spec §12.6; RD7a's Q1-Q3 are open): whether a lesson on a held LO is veiled until the check is answered. Not masked here, because what an opened veil writes is RD7's question | owner |

Not changed here (other workers' code, and the choice weighs revealing an answer against hiding the learner's own record) | owner |
| RD10-14 | RD10's fix round, the D15 sweep (owner decision) | Source titles are not masked anywhere: a video or book titled after a held LO is named in its window head, its shelf row, the path view's `book` and the timestamp block `alt+;` writes ("▶ <video title> at m:ss", from the window's own title, never from follow-along or the chapters). The goal lens's protection masks section, chapter and LO text only | none | Owner decision: whether a source title naming a held LO is protected (every surface showing a source title, and the timestamp block, which is stored in the learner's pad and outlives the check) | owner |

| RD9a-1 | RD9a's adversarial review (major) | A desk lesson request stayed in App after it was taken: DeskShell remounting (another tab and back) reset its seen-seq ref and opened the same lesson again, and a vault switch replayed the request in the new vault | `lesson/deskLessons.test.tsx` ("a request is taken once and the app told so…") | A request carries the vault it was asked in; DeskShell opens it only in that vault and tells App it was taken (`lessons.onConsumed`), and App forgets it then and on any vault change | fixed |
| RD9a-2 | RD9a's adversarial review (major) | An opening turn belonged to the view that sent it: a stack window closed while its opening was in flight and brought back by undo mounted a new view, which sent a second opening turn (two lessons, the tab showing the second under the first's id) | `lesson/lessonWindows.test.tsx` ("an opening closed and brought back…") | The opening's turn is owned by `LessonDesk.shareOpening`: sent once, shared by every view of the opening, and its answer settles the opening's lesson while no view is mounted | fixed |
| RD9a-4 | RD9a's adversarial review (major) | A lesson's notes could split across pads: a note written before `lesson.list` answered (or after it failed) took "not known yet" for "no pad" and created a second one; the read named only the newest, hiding the earlier notes; two clients could do the same | `test_reader_rd9a_fix.py::test_a_lesson_has_one_additions_pad_and_older_duplicates_are_all_named`, `lesson/lessonWindows.test.tsx` ("a note written before the lesson's summary lands…", "older additions pads … are shown too") | The sidecar refuses a second `lesson_additions` pad for a lesson inside the batch's transaction (`lesson_additions_exists`); `lesson.list` names the oldest as the lesson's pad and lists every one (`additions_pad_ids`); the desk creates no pad until the summary has landed (it reads it and says so), takes the stored pad over one made locally, and shows older duplicates' notes under the lesson | fixed |
| RD9a-5 | RD9a's adversarial review (major) | A failed step's recovery reads (`lesson_exists`, or an unavailable teacher with a saved lesson id) sent `lesson.view` at once from the catch, whatever the window's lifecycle and even after it unmounted; such a read can serve a ready question nobody sees | `lesson/lessonWindows.test.tsx` ("a failed step's recovery read waits for the window to be live…") | The read's lesson id is kept and the read made only while the view is mounted and live (`readWhenLive`); an unmounted view makes none | fixed |
| RD9a-6 | RD9a's adversarial review (major) | A lesson paper folded under "your additions" or scrolled out of its stack kept the stack's live lifecycle: it polled a question being prepared, and the read that found it ready claimed it served while hidden | `lesson/lessonWindows.test.tsx` ("a question being prepared is not checked on while its paper is folded…") | Each lesson paper has its own effective lifecycle (`PausedPart`, a small additive export of `lifecycle.tsx`): paused while folded or out of view (IntersectionObserver), so reads, probe polls and activity stop and its drafts stay | fixed |
| RD9a-7 | RD9a's adversarial review (major) | A finished TE2 lesson's review left out its quick question and appended the answer and result after everything else, the close card included | `test_reader_rd9a_fix.py::test_a_finished_te2_lesson_reviews_its_quick_question_in_recorded_order`, `lesson/lessonWindows.test.tsx` ("a finished lesson's quick question…") | `lesson.view` builds one feed in recorded order: a served question (as its item states it; nothing generated again) once answered or in a review, its answer and result where they happened; the window renders `probe_question` | fixed |
| RD9a-8 | RD9a's adversarial review (minor) | A finished lesson reopened (its review) still started an activity tracker, because closed views advertise `activity_log`, and wrote new activity rows while read, hidden or closed | `lesson/lessonWindows.test.tsx` ("a finished lesson's review records no activity…") | The tracker starts only once the view has shown the lesson open; one that closes while shown keeps its tracker, so its last interval is still sent | fixed |
| RD9a-3 | RD9a's adversarial review (major) | A TE2 quick question ("no notes or help") was not isolated in a window: the book beside it, "your additions" and the companion stayed usable, with only the lesson's reply box held. On the full screen nothing else was reachable | `test_reader_rd9a_fix.py::test_a_quick_question_is_never_served_or_answered_in_a_desk_window`, `lesson/lessonWindows.test.tsx` ("a lesson's quick question in a desk window…") | Interim, the owner's option (a) until RD7's administration guard exists: the quick question is administered on the full lesson screen, never in a desk window. A window's reads and steps say `frame: "window"`; the sidecar then neither serves nor shows a ready question (`status: waiting`, no first-serve claim) and refuses `answer_probe`. The tab says a quick question is waiting and opens the lesson screen, and the tab reads the lesson again when that screen closes. How an answer is recorded is unchanged. Options (b), a desk-wide guard, and (c), recording such answers as assisted, stay open for when RD7 is decided | fixed (interim; the owner may change it once RD7 is decided) |

## From the Reader workbench v2 batch 1 integration (2026-11-23; `docs/reviews/reader-batch-1-integration.md`)

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BATCH1-1 | the batch 1 integration, RD5a's menu backend meeting RD3's fix round | `passage.promoted_job` and `passage.promoted_cancel` called `PolledJobs.get/receipt/cancel` with a job id alone; RD3's fix round keys every polled job by vault (RD3-1), so with both merged every poll of the judged rows and every cancel raised a `TypeError` (the menu's judged rows never arrived and a closed menu's job was never cancelled) | `tests/test_menu_promoted_rows.py` (8 tests fail on the merged tree before the fix) | Both calls pass `vault_key(ctx)`, as the companion's asks and the ink reading do: a job id from another vault is not found | fixed |

## From the notebook frame and its packaged-app check (B4–B5, 2026-11-23; `docs/reviews/notebook-frame-b4-b5.md`)

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| NBF-1 | B4 (the merged tree, before this step's edits) | `DeskShell.test.tsx` ends with six unhandled rejections: the pads transport's `passageUnionRange` mock answers `undefined`, and `DeskShell.tsx`'s union read takes `union.spans` from it. The 90 tests pass; vitest reports the run as erroring | `npx vitest run src/screens/desks/DeskShell.test.tsx` (6 errors) | The test's union-read fake returns its spans (on the RD4 fix round's branch), merged here | fixed (`8344e01da6`) |
| NBF-2 | B4 visual reference (`#tabs=distance,pad=3`) | marimo's prose sits on a 25.2 px line; the mockup's lesson on 24 px (its size, measured by its line widths, is marimo's 14.4 px). marimo has no variable for either | `scripts/desk_harness/notebook.py` (prose at 14.4 px on 24 px) | Orchestrator, Nov 23: the mockup wins on looks. The theme is variables plus one rule: prose at the mockup's measured 14.4 px (its line widths match Literata at 14.4 px) on 24 px. The rule's first selectors (`p`, `li`) missed marimo's Markdown paragraphs, which it renders as `span.paragraph`; the harness had measured the wrong element. It now names `.paragraph` too, and the harness measures the paragraph that holds the text | fixed (notebook frame step; selectors completed in the pad cells step) |
| NBF-3 | B5 (the packaged app on a virtual display) | A runtime directory deeper than a socket's path allows (an `XDG_RUNTIME_DIR` of 60-odd bytes) refused every notebook as "too deep for a Unix socket's path" | `test_notebook_runtime::test_a_runtime_directory_too_deep_for_a_socket_is_passed_over` | `runtime_base_dir` passes over a runtime directory too deep for the socket's path for the temporary directory | fixed (notebook frame step) |
| NBF-4 | B7 (a lesson notebook's commit point) | `notebook.release` enabled a held cell but marimo runs an enabled cell only when it went stale while held: a held reveal whose inputs never changed (one that reads only `mo`) stayed blank after its release | `test_notebook_runtime_sandbox::test_a_lessons_held_cells_are_released_at_its_commit_point_and_not_before` (timed out waiting for the reveal before the fix) | the runtime runs the released cells after enabling them | fixed (this step) |
| NBF-5 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (BLOCKER) | The sandbox could write the host through the runtime's own files: notebook code replaced its writable `run_dir/token` with a symlink to a host file, and on restart the unsandboxed supervisor's `write_text` truncated and wrote that file (and `chmod` followed the link). The socket could be swapped for a symlink to a host socket, which the proxy then reached with requests the frame chose; a FIFO in the token's place hung the session | `test_notebook_host_boundary::test_a_trap_planted_from_inside_never_reaches_the_host` (symlink, hardlink, fifo, directory, socket: all five failed before the fix, the host file overwritten with the token and the host socket reached), `test_no_unsandboxed_call_site_touches_a_host_path_without_the_safe_helpers`, `test_the_sandbox_has_no_writable_host_path` | no writable host path in the sandbox; the token written by the host and mounted read-only; the socket created and listening on the host and passed as a descriptor; every host file through `notebook_runtime/hostfiles.py` (no-follow, exclusive creation through directory descriptors, type and owner checked; the socket checked before every connection) | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-6 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (major) | A pad's derived notebook was written through links: a link at `.learnloop/notebooks/pads` redirected the write and its replacement out of the vault, and a link at the predictable PID-based temporary name had its target truncated before the atomic replace | `test_notebook_pad_cells::test_the_derived_notebook_never_follows_a_link_out_of_the_vault` (the open succeeded through the link before the fix) | `hostfiles.write_beneath`: each directory below the vault opened without following links (a link component refused), a fresh unpredictable temporary created exclusively and never followed, replaced through the directory's descriptor (a link at the name is replaced, not written through) | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-7 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (major) | The session's secret path (the frame's capability) was passed as `--base-url /s/<secret>` in the launcher's and server's arguments, which any local user can read from `/proc/<pid>/cmdline`; with the port, both proxy checks pass and the proxy adds the upstream credential | `test_notebook_host_boundary::test_the_secret_path_is_not_in_any_process_arguments`, `::test_a_running_sessions_secret_is_in_no_command_line` (three processes carried the secret before the fix) | the base URL travels in the launcher's environment (readable only by this user) and the launcher adds it to marimo's in-memory argv, removing the variable so the kernel does not inherit it | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-8 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (major) | Frame JavaScript could grow the sidecar's memory without bound: every `set_ui_element_value` was kept in `session.ui_values` before the readiness check or the server's answer, for any object id (nonexistent controls too), and request bodies were unbounded; this memory is outside the session's caps | `test_notebook_runtime::test_an_oversized_request_is_refused_and_nothing_is_recorded_before_the_server_accepts_it`, `::test_recorded_values_are_only_known_controls_within_a_bound` | request bodies over `REQUEST_MAX_BYTES` are refused (413); values are kept (and read as commits) only after the server accepted them, only for controls of the notebook's own cells, and never past `UI_VALUES_MAX_BYTES` in all (both caps `keep`, the notebook's own size) | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-9 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (major) | A close or vault switch could miss a server being spawned: while `_launch` awaited the spawn, `stop` saw no process, finished, and the entry was forgotten; when the spawn returned, `_wait_ready` saw `_stopping` and `_launch` returned without killing the new server | `test_notebook_runtime::test_a_stop_during_a_spawn_kills_the_server_when_the_spawn_returns` (the spawned process was left running before the fix) | the supervisor tracks its start in flight and a stop waits for it; a spawn that returns after a stop, or a readiness wait ended by one, kills its server | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-10 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (major) | Closing could cancel cleanup before termination: the runtime allowed 15 s for every stop, while one supervisor's graceful shutdown, wait and SIGTERM/SIGKILL waits could take longer; then the loop's tasks were cancelled and the loop closed, so a server that stalled its shutdown and ignored SIGTERM survived | `test_notebook_runtime::test_closing_kills_a_server_that_stalls_its_shutdown_and_ignores_sigterm` (the process was left running before the fix) | one deadline for the graceful part; after it, every server still alive is sent SIGKILL from the calling thread (not a loop task, so it cannot be cancelled), on close and on a vault switch | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-11 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (major) | The sandbox's writable host directory had no enforced storage cap (the 8 MiB "bound" was a 30-second check of `READY` sessions, and stopped sessions kept their files); the sandbox's `/tmp` was an unsized tmpfs | `test_notebook_host_boundary::test_the_sandboxes_tmp_is_size_capped_like_its_scratch`, `::test_a_notebook_cannot_write_past_its_caps` (an 80 MiB write to `/tmp` succeeded before the fix) | no writable host directory (NBF-5); `/tmp` mounted again as a tmpfs at the scratch size (both charged to the scope's memory); the run-directory sweep and `RUN_DIR_MAX_BYTES` removed; a forgotten session's token, socket and run directory removed through `hostfiles` | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-12 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (major) | Without a systemd user manager the runtime fell back to `prlimit --as`, which bounds each process's address space, not the session's total memory, its tasks or its CPU (notebook code could spawn many children), and with no `prlimit` it ran with no limiter at all | `test_notebook_runtime::test_without_a_user_manager_notebooks_are_refused_not_run_under_a_per_process_limit` | no scope, no notebook: `missing_requirements` names the missing user manager (the runtime is unavailable, said in the frame) and `scope_prefix` refuses instead of falling back | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-13 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (major) | A lesson closed on another surface kept its notebook live: `closed_at` was read only at `notebook.open`, so an existing session stayed `review=False`, its commits counted and the frame went on releasing held cells | `test_notebook_lessons::test_a_lesson_closed_elsewhere_releases_nothing_more` (the status still read `review: false` before the fix), `test_notebook_runtime::test_a_session_marked_as_a_review_releases_nothing` | `notebook.status` and `notebook.release` read a lesson session's state again from the lesson's own record; once closed, `runtime.mark_review` makes it a review (no commit, no release); the frame stops asking because the session says review | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-14 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (major) | A crash brought back code the learner had replaced: `run_cells` updated only the session's memory, so a supervised restart loaded the snapshot the session opened with (`x = 1` after the learner ran `x = 2`), and reopening a live session did not refresh the snapshot either | `test_notebook_runtime::test_an_edited_cell_survives_a_restart_and_a_live_reopen_refreshes_the_snapshot` | a pad session keeps its cells as the snapshot holds them; a cell run rewrites the snapshot with the code that ran, and every reopen (live or not) writes the latest source | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-15 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (major) | A pad's frontend notebook state crossed vaults: the module registry was keyed by pad id only, so a pad of the same id in another vault (a copied vault) inherited `started` and mounted its frame, starting a kernel without its first ⇧↵, with the old status and pending runs | `PadCell.test.tsx` "a pad of the same id in another vault starts nothing of this vault's kernel" (it read "starting kernel…" before the fix) | the registry key is the pad's id in its vault (`padNotebookKey`: the desk store's client id, one per vault, with the pad id) | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-16 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (major) | Notebook Python holds the privileged marimo credential and can act as the sidecar: it reads the token, and, running in the kernel beside the server (same sandbox, same user), it can enable or run any of its own cells, during a review too, so `runtime._release`'s checks bind the frame, not the notebook. The records said full edit rights belong only to the sidecar | not reproduced as a test (a design limit, not a regression) | STOPPED with options (the record's Fix round): (a) state the limit and keep reveals free of anything protected (now in the records); (b) host-owned reveals, the reveal's content kept by the sidecar and sent into the kernel only after the commit (NA §6.3's design, an authoring-format change); (c) kernel and server in separate sandboxes, which does not help with (a)'s in-kernel access by itself. Records corrected | open (owner decision) |
| NBF-17 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (minor) | A pad's generated notebook bypassed the 2 MiB limit: pad block bodies are unbounded, and the generated bytes were written and opened without the check `_source` applies | `test_notebook_pad_cells::test_a_generated_notebook_larger_than_a_notebook_may_be_is_refused` (it opened before the fix) | the generated bytes are checked against `NOTEBOOK_MAX_BYTES` before anything is written or opened | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-18 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (minor) | Distinct block ids collided: `A` and `a` both became `c_a` (no hash), so two cells shared one function name and one runtime lookup; pad ids differing only in case shared a derived file name | `test_notebook_pad_cells::test_ids_that_differ_only_in_case_get_distinct_names` | an id that is not already its own lower-case identifier gets the exact id's hash; the generated names are checked unique before anything is written | fixed (fix round) |
| NBF-19 | Adversarial review of the notebook work on main (minor) | The frame did not retry: a transient open or status error set an error and ended polling, so recovery needed the manual button, against spec §11.2's three retries 1.5 s apart | `NotebookFrame.test.tsx` "retries a failed open three times, 1.5 s apart, before saying it failed", "after three retries the failure is said, with a try again" | a failed open or reading is retried three times, 1.5 s apart (`RETRY`, the spec's values), saying "trying again (n of 3)" with a frame already showing kept; then the failure and "try again"; the runtime being unavailable is said at once | fixed (fix round) |

## From the Reader workbench v2 batch 2 integration (2026-11-23; `docs/reviews/reader-batch-2-integration.md`)

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| BATCH2-1 | the batch 2 integration, RD8a's visual tool meeting the parity round's VR-46 | The comparison tool kept two stroke-width checks after the merges: the parity round's (equal within 0.05 px, the tool's rule for the mockup's 1/0.34 world sizes) and RD8a's older exact one. With VR-46's link stroke (4.70588 world px against the mockup's 4.7) the exact check reported a difference the tool's own rule calls equal: one extra row in RD2 and in RD3 | `visual_reference.py --stage rd2` and `--stage rd3` (22 and 8 rows; `ov_link stroke_width 4.7px / 4.70588px`) | The exact duplicate goes; the stroke's width, opacity and dashes compare once, by the tool's rule (RD2 21, RD3 7; rd8's pen strokes unchanged, 4 rows) | fixed |
| BATCH2-2 | the batch 2 integration, RD10's backend under the architecture test | `source_retrieval/video_chapters.py` writes the `video_chapters` table (replacing a reading's suggestions, a chapter's accept or "not this") but was not a registered SQL owner, so `test_sql_writes_stay_in_registered_owner_modules` failed with RD10 merged (it fails on RD10's own branch too) | `tests/test_architecture.py::test_sql_writes_stay_in_registered_owner_modules` | The module is registered beside the other source-retrieval owner, with what it writes and why there | fixed |

## From the Reader workbench v2 menus review (RD5, Codex Astra, 2026-11-23; frontend half of one fix round)

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| RD5-30 | RD5 review (major, `ContextMenu.tsx`) | A menu closed before `passage.promoted` answered never cancelled the judged job that answer named: the cleanup ran with no job id, and the late answer was dropped at the liveness check, so paid search and verification went on after the close | `ContextMenu.test.tsx > a menu closed before its first answer cancels the judged job that answer names` | A late first answer cancels its job at once | fixed |
| RD5-31 | RD5 review (major, `DeskShell.tsx`) | The source menu's rows for Open beside were cached by a wrapper around the calls, before the menu's liveness check: menu A's late answer, arriving after menu B's, overwrote B's rows, and B's Open beside opened A's correspondent | `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx > a closed menu's late answer never reaches the next menu…` | The menu tells its rows (cheap, then judged) through `onRows` only while it is open on its object; the wrapper is gone | fixed |
| RD5-32 | RD5 review (major, `DeskShell.tsx`, `verbs.ts`) | Promoted rows' actions were half wired: a margin note's row did nothing, a figure's row only toasted, a pad row lost its block, a pad line's gap row could not open What this knows (the dispatcher read the source menu's state), and every row was labelled `opened` whether or not anything opened | `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx > a pad row opens its pad at its line…`; `ContextMenu.test.tsx > a promoted row whose action could not run is not labelled opened` | The action runs for the object its menu was opened on: a pad row focuses its stack and flashes its line; a note's or figure's row opens its passage (the row's own when it names one, else the menu's); a gap row opens What this knows of that object; `opened` is recorded only after the action ran | fixed |
| RD5-33 | RD5 review (major, `DeskShell.tsx`, `verbs.ts`) | A selection across several passages gave every capture node the whole selection as its quote (which no single passage contains), and Append linked the line to the right-clicked passage only; the capture's own answer on where it landed was ignored | `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx > Append links the line to every passage the selection touches…`; `verbs.test.ts > Note in margin on a selection across passages…`; `model.test.ts > reads a passage, with the passages a selection over it touches` | The selection is cut at each passage it touches (`slices`): each capture node quotes its passage's own words; Append links the line to every touched passage; a highlight or note the capture could not place says so (and a highlight that could not be placed is not linked) | fixed |
| RD5-34 | RD5 review (major, `menu/model.ts`) | A selection inside a highlighted passage was dropped: the highlight's menu took the whole marked passage's text, so Ask, Append and Compare acted on other words than those selected | `model.test.ts > a selection inside a highlighted passage stays the selection…` | The highlight (or note) object keeps the selection, its passages and their slices | fixed |
| RD5-35 | RD5 review (major, `menu/model.ts`) | A window header's menu named no passage: both menu RPCs refuse a `window` object without one, so its promoted rows failed silently and What this knows errored; a panel's or thread's header had no reading at all | `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx > a reading's header asks about the passage in view…` | A reading's header is about the passage in view (the window's first visible passage); a window with no passage asks for no rows and offers its moves only (no What this knows) | fixed |
| RD5-36 | RD5 review (major, `DeskShell.tsx`) | A pad line's Teach me went through the app's `reader.onTeachMe` (the separate lesson screen on main); with RD9a merged it reaches the desk's lesson tabs only when the app passes that hook, and offers no Teach me without it | `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx > a pad line's Teach me opens a lesson tab in the desk's stack…` | The pads' Teach me calls `openLesson` itself, as the source menu does: one path, no app hook needed | fixed |
| RD5-37 | RD5 review (major, `PadOverlays.tsx`) | Teach me on a pad line linked to two readings sent the first reading's extraction with every link's span, so spans of the other reading went under the wrong extraction (span ids repeat across books) | `PadStackWindow.test.tsx > Teach me on a line linked to two readings…` | The lesson takes the line's passages in its first linked reading only | fixed |
| RD5-38 | RD5 review (major, `DeskShell.tsx`) | Show me wrote its figure into the running pad of the desk shown when the figure was ready (a poll answering after a desk switch landed it on the other desk), and the figure poll's registration was dropped: a job the sidecar no longer knew, or a window closed while paused, left a poll registered for ever | `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx > Show me lands the figure in the running pad of the desk it was asked on…`; `> a figure job the sidecar no longer knows ends its poll…` | The destination (the asking desk's running pad) is fixed when Show me is asked and checked when the figure lands; the poll keeps its removal and ends on a result, on `job_not_found`, and when its window is gone | fixed |
| RD5-39 | RD5 review (major, `DeskShell.tsx`) | ⊞ New desk from these ran gather to a new desk: it refused a window with no linked windows, and with links it moved up to four related windows instead of the window and its linked thread (spec §5.1, §9.2) | `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx > New desk from these moves the window alone…` | Its own operation: a new desk titled "new desk · <name>", the window moved there with its linked thread windows only, and focused | fixed |
| RD5-40 | RD5 review (major, `PanelWindow.tsx`, `verbs.ts`) | Compare opened a library window with the words only: asking there ran a library answer with no passage, so the comparison's intent and its anchor were lost | `LibraryPanel.compare.test.tsx > opened from a passage runs the comparison anchored on it…`; `verbs.test.ts > Compare opens a compare window…` | The menu's Compare keeps the passage (reading, spans, source, revision) in the window's view; asking there runs `compareFrom` through the selection's door, anchored on it (a plain library ask where comparing is off) | fixed |
| RD5-41 | RD5 review (major, `PadOverlays.tsx`) | The pad's Make it practice sheet kept "save to my queue" enabled while its request was on the way: a double press authored two practice items | `PadStackWindow.test.tsx > Make it practice saves once…`; `PadStackWindow.test.tsx > Make it practice sends one key per sheet…` | One save at a time (the button reads "saving…" and is disabled until the reply; a failure enables it again with its reason). A key per sheet (`clientIdempotencyKey`, RD5-5's) goes with every save of it: a retry after a lost reply returns the same item | fixed |
| RD5-42 | RD5 review (minor, `keys/router.ts`, `DeskShell.tsx`) | Bare `n` and `b` stayed marked for later and never ran; with a pad stack focused but the pointer over a passage, `.` returned the stack's failed hover menu instead of trying the hovered passage | `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx > n writes a margin note on the hovered passage; b runs its first promoted action…`; `> . with a pad stack focused but no line hovered…` | `n` opens the margin note on the hovered passage or cue (the menu's Note in margin); `b` runs the hovered object's first promoted row (labelled opened once it ran; its judged job is cancelled); `.` falls through to the hovered object when the stack has no hovered line | fixed |
| RD5-43 | RD5 review (minor, `ContextMenu.tsx`) | The Append submenu could not be used from the keys: with it open, the arrows still moved the parent menu and enter on Append only reopened it, so no pad was ever chosen by key | `ContextMenu.test.tsx > the Append submenu takes the keys while open…` | The submenu has its own selection (starting on the current pad): arrows, j/k and Tab move it, enter picks there, left or escape go back to the menu | fixed |
| RD5-44 | RD5 review (minor, `ContextMenu.tsx`, `KnowsPanel.tsx`, `PadOverlays.tsx`) | The menu's sheets and panel never took the focus and handled escape only on the dialog element, so escape pressed elsewhere did nothing; the pad's practice sheet had no escape at all; closing the menu never gave the focus back | `KnowsPanel.test.tsx > takes the focus when it opens…`; `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx > Note in margin's sheet takes the focus…`; `PadStackWindow.test.tsx > the Make it practice sheet takes the focus…` | One hook (`useSheetFocus`): a sheet takes the focus (its first field, else itself), escape closes it wherever the key lands, and the focus returns to where it was; the menu gives the focus back when it closes | fixed |
| RD5-45 | RD5 review (minor, `DeskShell.tsx`) | A promoted row's label that failed to send (a busy database) was swallowed: the row left the menu, the label was never stored, and the row came back next time | `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx > a label whose send failed is sent again under the same id…` | A label keeps its id until stored: a failed send is retried on the one poller with its backoff (labels are idempotent by id); one the sidecar refuses outright says the row may come back | fixed |
| RD5-46 | RD5 review (minor, `KnowsPanel.tsx`; the notes' half is the backend's) | What this knows left out the passage's adjustments and its linked notes, and showed highlights, figures and written questions with nothing to open | `KnowsPanel.test.tsx > shows the adjustments and the notes linked here…` | The adjustments (opening their thread) and the notes linked beside the passage are shown; a highlight, a figure or a written question opens its passage, where it shows. `passage.knows` hands the notes in since RD5-6 | fixed |

| RD5-48 | RD5 review (blocker, the client side of RD5-1) | The menu quoted and could send exam or held-out material: its header showed the words, and Ask, Find, Compare, Show me, Make it practice, Append, Carry and Teach me took them on, even before the server's answer | `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx > names the material without quoting it…`; `verbs.test.ts > exam or held-out material …` (two) | On `withheld`, the menu says plainly nothing is searched for it, names the material without quoting it, and leaves those verbs out; a verb that would send the words waits for the object's answer and refuses on protected material (as the pads refuse to copy it); the pad menu does the same for a line about protected material | fixed |
| RD5-49 | RD5 review (major, the client side of RD5-4) | An interrupted judged job could show rows from another vault's work | `ContextMenu.test.tsx > a judged job interrupted by a vault switch shows no rows of it…` | A job read as failed (`interrupted` included) shows none of its rows and says the menu can look again (a new opening asks anew) | fixed |
| RD5-50 | RD5 review (major, Make it practice on an exercise; RD5-8's option A) | Make it practice imported an exercise behind a yes/no: the learner never saw what would be made nor what was | `ExerciseSheet.test.tsx > shows the exercise's own words, imports only when asked…`; `> exam or held-out material: nothing of it shows…` | A sheet shows the exercise's own words (`passage.content`; protected material is neither shown nor imported), imports only when asked, and lists the items made with edit and retire, said to be in the queue already | fixed |

## Reader workbench, vault documentation review

| ID | Found in | Bug | Reproduction | Follow-up | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| WB-DOCS-1 | `screens/desks/DeskShell.tsx`, `menu/ContextMenu.tsx` | The source menu quotes the object while protection is pending. Its Append submenu calls `appendTo` directly, bypassing the normal verb's wait. A failed protection read also resolves the verdict as false, so ordinary Carry can store the selected text. | Static trace. Hold the first `passage.promoted` reply open for a protected passage and inspect the header or select an Append destination. For Carry, reject that first read. | `DeskShell.menu.test.tsx` holds, rejects and retries the first read and replaces a menu with an Append pending. Each opening owns its clearance. The header, shortcuts, pad actions and Append targets respect it. | fixed |
| WB-DOCS-2 | `screens/desks/menu/model.ts`, `handlers/passage_union.py` | The source menu checks the clicked span only while its verbs can take the whole selection. Carry can store words from a protected neighbor when the clicked span is clear. | Static trace. Select across an ordinary and a protected span, open the menu from the ordinary span, and follow `objectInput`, `_menu_places` and the Carry dispatch. | The verdict and text-bearing destinations check the clicked passage and every contributor, including source nodes in a margin capture and the ancestry of selected pad blocks. Delayed jobs carry the origin ids. `test_menu_protected_material.py` calls the destinations directly with mixed selections. | fixed |
| WB-DOCS-3 | `handlers/passage_union.py` | A completed promoted job can deliver a cached quote or related-passage row after its origin or target becomes protected. | Static trace. Finish the job before a mark changes and poll it after the change. `_promoted_job_payload` returns the stored rows without checking either end. | Finished jobs keep their origins and row dependencies. Every delivery reads current marks, drops newly protected rows and reports the origin verdict. `test_menu_protected_material.py` marks the origin, a linked target and a judged target after completion. | fixed |
| WB-DOCS-4 | Menu selection and delayed actions | A pad or notebook selection could use a single line's clearance for other words. An open comparison, companion chip, or figure landing could also lose the source that supplied its words. An older unplaced source quote could still be offered after a mark changed. | Select across two pad lines or open a second comparison with the same query from another passage. Inspect the origins at the destination. | The client retains the selection's source and pad ids through dispatch and completion. Ungrounded notebook text stays out of text-bearing actions. Older unplaced pad quotes need an empty protected set at use or delivery. Desk titles derived from comparison windows omit the query. Covered by the pad selection, notebook menu and verb tests. | fixed |
| WB-DOCS-5 | Promoted learner rows and What this knows | A carried question or margin note could quote a newly protected second passage from a menu opened on its still-clear first passage. | `test_carried_words_on_an_open_passage_follow_the_entire_saved_anchor` checks the initial rows, kept rows and What this knows after marking the second passage. | Text-bearing union entries carry their origins internally. Menu delivery checks those origins before returning the text. | fixed |

## Reader workbench copy follow-ups

| Id | Found in | Bug | Fail-first test | Fix | Status |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| COPY-1 | Copy audit §5.1 | The source menu can show a quote before protection is known. A failed lookup is treated as unprotected. The Append submenu can also copy the quote or use it as a new pad title. | Delay and fail the protection lookup for a protected selection, then attempt each Append path. | Menu worker owns the menu and its shell integration. Use neutral headers and require an explicit safe result for all copying paths. | fixed (WB-DOCS-1, menu branch; reviewed in batch 5) |
| COPY-2 | Copy audit §5.2 | The passage union returns stored quotes and authored questions on protected rows, including links to protected destinations. The passage-details panel renders these rows. | Read a stored quote and question on a protected passage, then read a link to that passage from an eligible one. | Apply protection to union rows and their destinations before returning them. The menu worker owns panel rendering. | fixed (BATCH5-3) |
| COPY-3 | Copy audit §5.3 | Masking for due cold checks is in place, but a chapter saved before a source or range is reclassified as exam or held-out material can still return its title and topic tags. | Save and accept a chapter, reclassify its source or range, then read list, job, decision and follow-along replies. | Apply current source and range protection on every saved chapter delivery and invalidate affected rendered titles. | fixed (BATCH5-5) |
| COPY-4 | Copy audit §5.5 | The goal band, goal chooser and unmatched rows receive raw names and phrases outside the protection applied to path sections and topics. Neutral rendering obscures legitimate labels too. | Set one held and one unrelated phrase in a goal, open the band, chooser, path and reason card, then verify only the unrelated label is shown. | Provide protection-aware labels for the chooser, goal and unmatched display, keeping action payloads available. | fixed (BATCH5-4) |
| COPY-5 | Copy audit §2.2–2.4 | The in-memory desk outbox can drop a rejected or conflicting operation and report settled. Figure insertion can also discard an immediate refusal. Pad feedback cannot confirm the specific write. | Reject one pad operation during each of Append, diagram insertion, reply copy, lift, and drain; verify that each result reflects that write. | Surface per-operation outcomes and use them for save and failure feedback, including figure insertion. | open |
| COPY-6 | Copy audit §2.4 | Companion Retry can bypass the desk-write wait applied on the first ask. | Fail the initial desk write, click Retry while a required edit is unresolved, and check that no question is sent early. | Route retries through the same readiness gate as the first request. | open |
| COPY-7 | Copy audit §2.11 | Carried-question mark actions can resolve with `applied: false` while the shelf and passage marks treat resolution as success. | Send a stale expected version for a carried question, then verify that it remains visible and can be retried. | Inspect `applied` before removing a row or a mark. | open |
| COPY-8 | Copy audit §4.3 | The due-check button has no destination without a host. Failures looking up a path in the background can be silent. | Open a goal with a due, unhosted question, then simulate a rejected background path request. | Route the check button to Today when needed and show a recoverable path lookup failure. | open |
| COPY-9 | Copy audit §2.13 and §4.11 | Failed chapter listing produces the same empty state as an empty list. Chapter cancel failure is silent. | Reject the chapter list and cancel requests separately while leaving the video open. | Track and display recovery for each failed operation. | open |
| COPY-10 | Copy audit §2.11 and §1.1 | A failed practice-topic lookup looks empty. The union returns topic identities without protection-aware titles. | Compare a failed lookup, an empty reading and a reading with held and eligible topics. | Carry separate lookup outcomes and supply safe topic labels for the existing options. | open |
| COPY-11 | Copy audit §2.14 | The lesson’s generic busy state covers writing, grading and other actions. | Submit an answer, send a reply and open a goal, then check each pending label. | Use the active action to show grading-specific feedback when grading is pending. | fixed (BATCH5-1) |
| COPY-12 | Copy audit §4.1 | A desk load that fails still leaves the opening screen mounted. | Reject the initial desk request, then retry from the opening screen. | Give the loading screen an error state and inline recovery using the existing Reader navigation or a retry. | open |
| COPY-13 | Copy audit §5 follow-up | The Companion shortcut can quote a selection in its chip before the question RPC checks protection. If a protected passage is still visible in Reader, the chip can repeat it. | Select protected text in a visible Reader window and open the companion with C and Shift+C before making any request. | Use a neutral selection label until the caller has an explicit safe protection result. | open |
